Uniforms


Briefcase		
	
	
SUS+1
Holds one Slot Size 12 Weapon or two item slots.   
Removing items from a Brief Case takes 3 Combat Actions.  
Handheld Item.  
Requires at least one vacant Weapon Slot. 
Optional 3-digit locking mechanism.  
Briefcase SUS modifier ignored when wearing Business Suit.
Purse/Satchel	
	
SUS+1
Holds one Slot Size 6 Weapon or one item slot.   
Removing items from a Purse takes 1 Combat Action. 
Requires at least one vacant Weapon Slot. 
SUS modifier ignored if Character is Female.
Guitar Case	
	
	
SUS+1
Holds one Slot Size 14 Weapon or two item slots.  Removing 
items from a Guitar Case takes 3 Combat Actions.  Handheld Item. 
Requires at least one vacant Weapon Slot.
Backpack	
	
	
SUS+2
Holds one Slot Size 15 Weapon or two item slots.   
Removing items from a Backpack takes 4 Combat Actions.  
Requires at least one vacant Weapon Slot. 
Backpack SUS modifier ignored when wearing Tourist, Pack 
Rat, or Hobo Uniform. 
Utility Belt	
	
	
SUS+1
Increase your Small Items capacity from 6 to 12. 
Dyneema Gloves	
	
SUS+1
Your hands do not take damage from bladed weapons or 
other sharp items.  Add +2 to Disarm when attempting to 
disarm bladed weapons away from an enemy.
Can be stored in one Item Slot.
Combat Boots	
	
SUS+0
When kicking an enemy, increase your damage by +1D6.
Your feet are protected from most damage (Director’s discretion). 
Reduce Move Speed by 1.  
Army Helmet	 	
	
SUS+2
Mutilations that mention your head and face (Scar, Disfig-
ured, Lost Eye, Broken Neck, Decapitation) have a 2 in 6 
chance of breaking your Army Helmet and nullifying the 
mutilation effect.
Can be stored in two Item Slots.
Kevlar Vest	
	
	
SUS+1
Reduce incoming damage by 3, and Move Speed by 1.
(Not compatible with Plate Carrier, or Tactical Armor)
Plate Carrier	 	
	
SUS+2
Reduce incoming damage by 5, and Move Speed by 1.
(Not compatible with Kevlar Vest, or Tactical Armor)
Tactical Armor		
	
SUS+3
Reduce incoming damage by 8, and Move Speed by 1.
(Not compatible with Kevlar Vest, or Plate Carrier)
Night Vision Goggles		
SUS+2
You can see in the dark, but can not see in bright light.  
Can be stored in one Item Slot.
You can not Aim firearms. 
Requires one Action to activate or deactivate.  
Thermal Vision Goggles	SUS+2
You can see any Character that gives off heat within 20 
Units; however, you can not see details or identify that 
Character.   
Can be stored in one Item Slot.
You can not Aim firearms. 
Requires one Action to activate and deactivate.  
Gas Mask		
	
	
SUS+3
You are immune to toxic gases.
Can be stored in one Item Slot.
Reduce Awareness rolls by 2.
You can not Aim firearms.
Requires one Action to activate and deactivate. 
Black Cape		
	
	
SUS+2
Stealth gets +3 while in dark areas. 
Can be stored in one Item Slot.
SKI MASK	 	
	
	
SUS+3
Helps you stay warm in cold weather and masks your identity.
Can be stored in one Item Slot.
Pistol Holster	
	
SUS+4
Holds one Slot Size 5 Weapon.  Removing items from Holster 
takes zero Combat Actions.  
Requires at least one unused Weapon Slot. 
Pistol Holster SUS modifier ignored when wearing Lab Coat 
or Trench Coat Uniforms.

The following is a list of common items and goods that may be valuable to Characters.  Characters may carry up to eight “Every 
Day Carry” (EDC) items.  EDC item slots are listed on the front of your character sheet.  “Small Items” are listed on the back of 
your character sheet.  Worn items such as t-shirts and wristwatches do not use up inventory spaces.  Larger items may take up 
multiple Slots.  “Handheld” items must be carried by hand at all times.  You can choose to equip your Character with any of the 
items listed below, provided you have enough item slots.  “OTU” items are One Time Use and must be removed from your sheet 
after utilized.  Some OTU items have a limited number of uses, such as a book of matches can be used 20 times.

148
Items


Choose your Weapons 
Select the weapons you carry in your Weapon Slots.  Weap-
ons kept within these slots are considered concealed until 
drawn.  All weapons are used two-handed by default.  See 
“Object Weight” on page 75 for more information on 
holding weapons one-handed.  All firearms utilize gener-
ic ‘Bullets’ for ammunition, unless otherwise specified.  The 
Mass of firearms must not exceed the capacity of the Weap-
on Slot they occupy.   Special rules are indicated by key-
words (refer to the adjacent page).
Note:  Weapons with a Mass of 2 or less can also be car-
ried in an Item Slot (Every Day Carry).
You have the creative freedom to name your firearms as you 
see fit, whether choosing real-world equivalents, the fictional 
names listed below, or inventing unique names.  In SCP.2e, 
weapons with more power generally have higher Mass.  While 
this may not always align with reality, it enhances gameplay in 
this tabletop setting.  Additionally, the real life equivalent of the 
guns can be anything the Director or PC feels is appropriate.  It 
doesn’t matter if the gun is a pistol or assault rifle, the stats are 
the only factor as far as game mechanics.
Weapons will list of stats below.  If you would like a simple ex-
planation on how these mechanics work together, simply check 
out the “Fire” or “Melee Attack” Combat Actions on page 
“Melee Attack” on page 63.
Weapon Name
The default name of the weapon.  Weapons can be renamed 
to whatever you’d like.  Weapons can be described to look like 
anything within reason, for example the “XR 7” could be de-
scribed as a pink KA assault rifle with heart stickers all over it, 
or be described as a sleek gold plated M16 with SCP insignia.
To-Hit
This modifier is always applied to the To-Hit roll when firing 
this weapon.  
Aim
This modifier is added to the To-Hit roll if the Aim Action is per-
formed (see page 63).  Most weapons can only have the 
Aim Action performed up to one time before firing, but weap-
ons with “Aim+” can be aimed twice, and weapons with “Snip-
er” can be aimed up to three times.
Recoil
After a gun is fired it has recoil that effects subsequent shots 
on the same Combat Round.  If you fire a gun as your fi-
nal action, or you fire a gun only once on your Turn, you 
never have to worry about recoil modifiers.  If you fire 
a gun twice in a row on the same Turn, then the second 
shot has it’s To-Hit roll reduced by the Recoil of the gun. 
B.Dam
Stands for “Base Damage.”  If you hit an enemy with an attack, 
you always deal the Base Damage to your opponent.
X.Dam
Stands for “X Damage.”  X Damage is multiplied by either your 
Combat Rating (for melee attacks) or your Projectile Rating (for 
shooting attacks) and is bonus damage that is included along 
with your Base Damage after you successfully hit your target. 
Higher Strength Ratings will increase your damage with Melee 
weapons, while a higher Perception Rating will increase your 
damage with firearms.  
Mag
How many times the weapon can be fired before it needs the 
magazine changed using a “Use Item/Reload” Action.  
Players:  If your Director is running a survival horror type 
game where ammo is scarce, then you should keep a close eye 
on how many rounds you have left in your guns.  Otherwise, if 
your campaign is not survival focused and bullets are plentiful, 
then you should not bother to keep close tabs on your ammuni-
tion unless your guns magazine has very few bullets (less than 
5).  Players need only monitor their overall ammunition count 
and need not concern themselves with the specific number of 
clips they possess.
Directors:  Be aware that keeping track of ammo detracts from 
the heat of combat and you shouldn’t punish players for not 
being hyper focused on a tally of their ammo.  
Range
Another modifier to your To-Hit roll.  Divide the number of Units 
between you and your enemy by your Range, and subtract 1 
to your To-Hit roll by this amount.  For example, if your Range 
was 6, anything 6 Units and under wouldn’t have a modifier. 
An enemy that is 7-12 Units away would get a -1 modifier to 
the To-Hit roll.  13-18 Units away would have -2, and so forth. 
If the weapons Range has an asterisk (*) after the number, then 
it means that the weapons range stops short against targets past 
that distance.  Melee weapons, for example, typically have a 
Range of 1*.  This means that the weapon can hit enemies up to 
one Unit away, but can’t reach past that distance.
Special
Keywords and special rules that are clarified on the adjacent page.
Mass
How large and heavy the weapon is.  A weapon can only be 
stored in a “Weapon Slot” of an equal or greater Slot Size (see 
Uniforms on page 146).  If you can’t carry a weapon in one 
of your Weapon Slots, you must carry it in your hand(s).

GUNS
Weapons & Gear
150
Guns


All firearms utilize generic ‘Bullets’ for ammunition, unless otherwise specified.  Bullets and Shells (for shotguns) are free 
for the taking while at your home base.  Players can store 25 bullets or 10 shells in a Small Item slot, or up to 100 bullets 
or 40 shells inside of one Every Day Carry slot.  Characters do not need to keep track of how many Magazines they carry. 
Additionally, Players and Directors alike should only casually keep track of ammo unless the situation makes ammo scarce.
Some weapons will list Keywords in their Special Rules column that provide rule alterations to the guns mechanics.  
3-R Burst
If your To-Hit roll succeeds by 3 or more, add +2 Base Damage Dice.  
Shooting reduces Magazine by 3 rounds. 
Aim+
Normally Characters may only Aim once before firing.   Aim+ allows you to Aim twice before firing.
Automatic
Add +1 damage for each point To-Hit roll succeeds by.  Automatic weapons also allow the option to “Spray 
and Pray” (see page 65).  Special Note:  You cannot perform a “Called Shot” using Automatic fire.
Shooting reduces Magazine by 5 rounds.
Lightweight
Reduce this weapon’s Draw Actions by 1.
Silencer
This gun has a sound suppressor attached which greatly reduces the sound when firing.
Shotgun
Gun uses “Shells” for ammo instead of Bullets.  When calculating Range modifiers, instead of subtracting 
from the To-Hit roll, you add to the To-Hit roll and also reduce the final damage by one die.  For example, 
if your Range was 5 and your target was 12 Units away, 5 goes into 12 two times, and therefore the To-Hit 
roll would get a +2 and the final damage would roll 2 less dice.  If a Mutilation occurs from a shotguns fired 
at point blank (one Unit away) include the “Limbloss” Mutilation as a possibility.
Sniper
Normally Characters may only Aim once before firing but Sniper allows you to Aim up to three times before 
firing, however you must prepare the shot by resting the gun on a ledge and/or going prone.  
Preparing a sniper rifle to zero in on your target takes one full combat round (2 actions).  
Sniper Rifles can not be held one-handed.
Continue to the next page for Melee Weapons...

Weapons & Gear
152
Blades


Some weapons will list Keywords in their Special Rules column that provide rule alterations to the blades mechanics.  
Assassin
If attacking a Staggered Character, increase all Damage Dice by +1 Die Type (D6’s become D8’s etc)
Bonecrushing
Opponents Critically Hit with this weapon automatically become Staggered.
Compartment
Weapon contains a secret compartment (with optional locking mechanism) that can hold three items, or 
another weapon with Mass of 3 or less.
Disguised
Weapon does not appear to be a weapon (Cane, Briefcase, Umbrella, etc)  
No SUS modifiers are applied due to weapon being drawn.
Lashing
Damage dice that roll their maximum result are doubled.
Mechanized Retraction
Reduce Draw Actions by 1.
Oversized
Attacking requires two consecutive Actions.  Adds +2 SUS.
Throwing Weapons
Throwing Weapons deal both Base Damage and X Damage when the object is thrown.  Include the weapons To-Hit modifier 
when rolling To-Hit, unless it is a negative number.  Character uses the higher of either Combat or Projectile ratings when 
multiplying X Damage.
See “Throwing Objects” on page 74.
See example on page 176.
Twin Symmetry
You may target all Characters within weapon range with each attack.  
Special Attack, Special Melee Attack, and Special Firearm Attack Rules (see page 64) can not be applied 
to attacks that target multiple Characters.
Continue to the next page for Special Weapons...
Weapons & Gear
153
Blades


Despite their unique properties, these weapons are accessible to Player Characters under standard conditions at their home 
base.  Weapons listed here have special rules or characteristics that require minor clarification beyond keywords.
Special Weapons
After removing the pin, Flashbang will detonate at the end of the Combat Round.  All Characters within 8 Units of 
the grenade must roll Intellect + Awareness difficulty 15 or they become nearly blinded for (5 Minus their Resilience) 
Combat Rounds.  While nearly blind, reduce all Perception and Dexterity rolls by 5.
Requires 1 Item Slot
Flashbang 
After pulling the pin, the Concussion Grenade detonates at the end of the Combat Round. All Characters within 6 
Units of the grenade when it detonates get -2 to all Skill checks on their following Turn.  In addition, they must make 
a Willpower + Self-Control roll with a difficulty of 15.  Failure results in a penalty of losing two Combat Actions 
on their next Turn.  This penalty decreases by one action for every 7 points of their Willpower Rating + Resilience.
Requires 1 Item Slot
Concussion Grenade
When Caltrops are spread onto a Unit, Characters that travel through that Unit reduce their Move Speed by 1 until 
the end of the Combat Round and take 1D4 damage.

Chapter Twelve:  
The Example Chapter
This chapter will attempt to encapsulate many rules of SCP.2e 
within a single narrative and demonstrate the flow of every-
thing from character creation, to combat, to how a role-play-
ing game should sound.  This story is about people just like 
you, getting ready to play the SCP.2e.  The Director’s name 
is David.  David wishes to play with three of his friends- 
Amanda, Billy, and Charles.  The four all agree to meet up 
and talk about the game they will all be taking part in.
Part 1:  Conceptualization
After reading SCP.2e, David has an idea for a campaign.  He 
wants to involve one of his favorite SCPs and create a campaign full 
of mystery, combat, and pit the PCs up against a seemingly unstop-
pable force.  After coming up with a basic idea for the story he gets 
to work, and then looks at the checklist beginning on page 98.
David looks over the options for SDC and CDL, then chooses to 
go with a Vlam ±1 game, which will allow any Disruption Class 
except for Ekhi and Amida.  This will allow his players to have a 
flexibility in character creation, but David doesn’t want overpow-
ered PCs right from the get go.  He plans on running a Long Cam-
paign which will last several months, but he wants a series of short 
stories so he has the option to quit early or extend the game longer. 
He also opts for a “Low Level Grunts” type of game, but he hopes 
his players will succeed in a few missions and become respected 
among their peers.  When looking at options for Anomalous As-
pects, David doesn’t want to commit to a definitive yes or no, so 
he decides to choose “Limited Anomalous” and allow for maybe 
one or two players to buy something a little weird, but he doesn’t 
want a whole group of anomalous players.  After reading through 
the Campaign Lethality section, David decides to go with a mix of 
Cosmic Horror, Deadly Encounters, and Common Combat.  He 
figures he will throw the PCs into several different scenarios so the 
lethality of the campaign isn’t necessarily set in stone.  For the SCP 
Canon David decides to go with a typical SCP universe (if there 
is such a thing) where there are several SCP sites throughout the 
globe and most SCPs on the wiki exist to some extent.  He chooses 
the game to take place in the year 2024 in Nevada, USA.  In his 
universe the SCP Foundation is military strict, but when not in the 
heat of danger employees sometimes goof around and have fun. 
Players will have the standard items available to them and he will 
not restrict starting gear.  Finally, David decides to allow for any 
method of character creation, an Unusual Phenomena Scale of 
“Global Foundation,” and David decides not to incorporate Dra-
ma Cards for his story.  
He then types up the following Introductory Paragraph:
“It is the year 2024 and the SCP Foundation maintains a 
secret network of facilities to contain anomalous entities 
and objects worldwide.  Your Characters have worked 
for various branches of the SCP Foundation for several 
years.  Although you all know each others names, you 
only have had a few ‘water cooler’ discussions so you 
don’t know each other that personally.  Each of you are 
Security Level 02, C-Class personnel by default.  You 
work at SCP Site-112.  Site-112 is located in the Black 
Rock Desert of northwest Nevada.  Site-112 is a generic 
SCP containment site that specializes in Euclid anoma-
lies which can emanate psychic waves or radio signals 
short distances, and must be kept far away from civi-
lization.  There is a massive security gate on constant 
watch which encompasses Site-112 in a 16 kilometer 
radius.  Your Site Director’s name is Director Newark, 
who has been at the facility since its inception almost 
a decade ago.  Your characters will begin the game 
at Site-112 in various positions, but recently have been 
recruited to work together for a special field-mission, of 
which you have been given no details.  This is a Vlam ±1 
campaign and if you would like to buy any anomalous 
aspects, ask me first because they will be limited if I get 
too many requests.”
Satisfied with his introduction, Dave is ready to help his friends with 
character creation.
His three friends meet up at Dave’s house every Saturday around 
7pm each week.
Saturday comes...
Part 2:  Character Creation
Charles:  “Is it going to be a problem if I don’t know much 
about the SCP universe?”
David:  “Not a problem at all.  All you need to know is that 
you work at a facility that contains weird artifacts and crea-
tures.  It’s a modern setting, and the SCP Foundation has 
nearly limitless funds to help contain the anomalies.  They are 
also a global organization.”
Charles:  “I know the basics, but I don’t know like what all 
the SCPs do and stuff.”
David:  “Nobody does!  (laughs)  If you know the very basics 
you’ll be fine.  I’ll slowly introduce you to the more specifics 
and you’ll catch on as we play.”
Amanda:  “I’ve played this game before.  Okay if I use ad-
vanced character creation?”
David:  “Yes.”
Billy:  “Can I be an SCP?”
David:  “Not this time.  The disruption classes are limited so 
you can’t be Ekhi or Amida”
Billy:  “Oh, I was kidding anyway.  I’ll just be a D-Class and 
die the first session.”
David:  “I’ll allow that.”
The Example Chapter
156
Introductory Paragraph


Amanda:  “So did you print out character sheets?”
David:  “Yes!  Here you go!”  
David hands out pre-printed character sheets to his friends.
David:  “Okay so if you guys want you can pick from the 
pregenerated characters from the pdf I shared earlier, or you 
can use the fast character creation method.  If you already 
played this game before or are feeling confident, you can use 
advanced character creation, but I only suggest that if you 
want to take a bit longer to make a more custom character.”
Amanda:  “I’d like to do that, yes.”
David:  “No problem, what about Billy and Charlie?”
Billy:  “I’ll do fast character creation.”
Charles:  “Can I just pick one of the premade guys?”
David:  “Sure!  Let’s get started.  First Charles.  Go through 
the premade characters pdf and find a character you’d like to 
play.  Since this is a Vlam plus one game, you can not choose 
any of the premade characters that list Ekhi or Amida as their 
Disruption Class.”
Charles looks through the premade Characters.
Charles:  “I like the stats for Sarah Gijsel, but can I rename 
them to Norton Spetz?”
David:  “Of course!  You can also change their title and even 
swap out their items if you want.”
Charles:  “I’ll keep everything else the same but I want to be 
Norton Spez.”
David:  “Okay copy the stats to your sheet, and next I’ll help 
Billy.”
Charles copies the premade 
stats of Sarah Gijsel to his 
printed character sheet and 
is finished.
David: “Okay Billy you’re next.  First you will copy the char-
acter info on page 18, and then come up with your name.”
Billy begins to copy the data.  He names his Character D-42069
Billy:  “It says C Class is default but I wanted to start as a D-Class.”
David: “That’s fine.  Fill in a D for the Class and a zero 
for your Security Level, then just make sure you choose the 
D-Class for the next step.”
Billy:  “How do I do that?”
David:  “You’ll find the D-Class...class on page 36, just flip 
to that page and copy all the information there to your sheet.”
Billy:  ...”done.”

David:  “Okay now you get to fill in ten of the dice icons for 
your attributes.  After you fill the dice in, for the SCP circle at 
top right of each of your attributes, write 
the number of total dice icons filled in.”
Billy:  “Do I also count the two dice that 
are already filled in?”
David:  “Yes, so each of your attributes 
will have at least a rating of 2.”
Billy fills in ten dice and then counts how 
many dice are filled in for each attribute, and 
records each attribute rating.
David:  “Next I’ll need you to go to 
page 28 and follow the steps to calculate 
the rest of your stats.  Let me know if you 
have any questions.”
Billy’s Character has a Dexterity Rating of 4.
The book says that his Speed Rating is his Dexterity Rating divided 
by 2, rounded up, plus 2.
Billy fills in a 4 for his Speed Rating.
Billy’s Character has a Strength Rating of 5.
The book says that his Strength Rating divided by 2 rounded up is 
how you calculate the Combat Rating.
5 divided by 2 is 2.5, rounded up is 3.
Billy fills in a 3 for his Combat Rating.
Billy’s Character has a Perception Rating of 4.
The book says that his Perception Rating divided by 2 rounded up 
is how you calculate the Projectile Rating.
4 divided by 2 is 2.
Billy fills in a 2 for his Projectile Rating.
Billy’s Character has a Willpower Rating of 3.
The book says that his Willpower Rating divided by 2 rounded up 
is how you calculate how much Exertion he has.
3 divided by 2 is 1.5, rounded up is 2.  Exertion begins with one 
dash already filled in.
Billy fills in a total of three Exertion.
Billy’s Character has a Health Rating of 4.
The book says to multiply your Health Rating by 5, then add 8
4 times 5, plus 8, is 28.
Billy fills in 28 for his Max HP, and then also fills in he has 28 HP.

Reaction is equal to his Dexterity Rating (4) plus his Dodge skill (2), 
while Stagger is equal to his Fate (2) Rating plus his Poise skill (0).

tokens.  She adds another +8 to her base HP (which is now 16) 
records her Base Speed of 3, and then flips her sheet around to 
record 20 History points.  Her final values are as follows:

Cyborg Eye
Your character has one of their eyes augmented 
with futuristic cybernetics.  Mutilations that cause 
permanent disfigurement to your face or eyes are 
ignored.  Character has the capability to optically 
zoom their eye to see distant objects.  Character also 
can see in the dark, but the eye gives off a dim red 
glow at all times.  Add +3 to SUS (unless wearing an 
eye patch).  Add a +2 To-Hit bonus for your first Aim 
Action with any firearm.

David looks at the Aspect, and decides that as long as she can 
come up with an interesting story behind it, he doesn’t see a prob-
lem with it.
David:  “Sure!  Just come up with some history behind it. 
Perhaps you had a history with Anderson Robotics, or had a 
cybernetic specialist as a friend in the Foundation.”
Amanda:  “I was thinking maybe while in the Marines I got 
my eye shot out, and while in critical condition the government 
had some experimental robotics engineer implant my eye, 
who was looking for a volunteer but nobody wanted to have 
their eye gouged out for the experiment, so they just went 
through with the procedure on me, which also saved my life.”
David:  “That sounds interesting!  Come up with the name of 
the engineer and I’ll keep that in mind for the future.”
Amanda:  “How about Dr. Wiley?”
David:  “Okay!”

Next Amanda chooses a Uniform.  She decides to go with Cover-

Amanda then finds three weapons that can fit into her Uniforms

She is now on the final steps of Character Creation.  Her 6 History 
Points are converted into 6 Skill Points.  At this step, she can not 
increase any skill that is over half of its governing attribute.  For ex-
ample, her Brawn skill is 3.  Brawn is governed by Strength.  Aman-
da’s Strength Rating is only 4.  This means she can not spend any 
Skill Points to increase her Brawn any further.
She decides to increase her Awareness Skill from a 1 to a 2.  This 
costs 2 Skill points.  She then increases it again from 2 to 3, which 
costs her 3 more Skill points.  She has just one point left and decides 
to buy the Computers skill at level 1 (which costs 1 Skill point).
Lastly, she calculates her remaining stats.
After Dividing her Dexterity Rating (5) by her DEX divider (4) and 
rounding up, she adds her Base Speed of 3.  
5 divided by 4 is 1.25, round this number up and 
you get 2.    Amanda’s Base Speed is 3 (which 
was determined by her age).  This means her final 
Speed Rating is 5.
She divides her Strength Rating (4) by her STR 
divider (4) rounded up.  4 divided by 4 is 1.
The result makes her Combat Rating a 1.
She divides her Perception Rating (6) by her PER 
divider (4).  6 divided by 4 is 1.5, round this number 
up and you get 2.  The result makes her Projectile 
Rating a 2.
She divides her Willpower Rating (5) by her WIL divider (4). 
5 divided by 4 is 1.25, round this number up and you get 2.
Exertion begins with one dash already filled in.  Amanda fills in two 
additional dashes for a total of 3 Exertion.
Amanda’s Health Rating is 5, multiplied by her HTHx of 3 gives her 
15, which is then added to her Base HP of 16.  Her Max HP comes 
out to 31.  She fills in this for both her Max HP and her current HP.
Amanda doesn’t have a Dodge Skill, and her Dexterity Rating is 5. 
This makes her Reaction score 5.
She has a Fate Rating of 4, and a Poise Skill of 1.  This makes her 
Stagger score a 5 as well.

panying Isabella and Norton, and if they don’t attempt any 
funny business there is a possibility this could eliminate their

uals, responsible for the breach in security.  You three will be 
provided with a secure communication device to contact me 
using encoded messages.  This device will accompany you to 
Site-100, however there may be some complications if you 
don’t get along with it.”

the six masks earlier itemized.  Each of the characters keep

are Adam, Francis, Isabella, Jasper, Norton, and Taylor.  The 
script looks like it’s had several rewrites, and in the corner of 
the last page you can see someone scribbled down ‘2026 
version’ in red ink.”
Charles:  “Wait, what year was it again?”
David: “Last you recall it was 2024.  Okay back to Billy.  You

Part 5: Combat!
Charles:  “How do I do that?”
David: “Everyone rolls Intellect plus Awareness!”
Charles:  “Awareness has PER after it, shouldn’t we be rolling 
Perception?”
David: “You can roll different Attributes with each skill, it’s 
just that those listed after them are the most common ones 
used for that skill.  But with initiative rolls you always roll Intel-
lect dice and add your Awareness.”
Charles has 4D6 Intellect dice and an Awareness of zero.  
He scores an 8.
Billy has 2D6 Intellect and an Awareness of 1.
He also scores an 8.
Amanda rolls 2D8 and 1D6 for Intellect, and her Awareness is 3, 
but she also has a times 3 multiplier, so her Awareness is effectively 
9.  She rolls her dice and her top two add up to ten.
Amanda:  “19!”
David: “Can anyone beat a 19?  No?  Okay.  So the pillar 
sprays red blood all over Adams face.  After it was stabbed, 
a deep growl echoes throughout the room, and a black whirl-
pool of dark energy breaches through into your dimension. 
Through this portal a creature emerges.  It is almost seven feet 
tall, highly muscular, has wings and it’s head is shaped al-
most like a pterodactyl.  You can’t tell if it’s absorbing light, 
or if it’s skin is just that black.  It’s nearly impossible to discern 
any additional details, other than the fact that it’s very hostile. 
Amanda you scored highest, so you get to decide who acts 
first in combat.”
Amanda:  “I choose myself, and I’m running to the double 
doors.”
David: “It’s about 7 meters away from your current position. 
What’s your move speed?”
Amanda: “It’s a 5.”
David: “That will mean you have to use two move actions to 
get up to it.  Is that what you want to do?”
Amanda:  “Yes.  And then I pass the turn to Billy.”
David: “Okay Billy, the black winged humanoid is only 4 
meters, or units, away from you.  What do you do?”
Billy:  “My move speed is 4, and my knife is already in my 
hand, so couldn’t I move and then stab it?”
David: “Yes, you can do that, and you could even get a bo-
nus to your damage using the momentum attack rules.”
Billy:  “Okay!  I do that.  Also I have 3 points of Exertion. 
Can’t I use those points to increase my damage somehow?”
David: “Yes, you can spend Exertion to add to your To-Hit 
roll, or spend it to increase your combat rating, which aids 
in damage.  But I will say that you should wait to erase your 
Exertion until after you hit.  You don’t spend the Exertion until 
just before damage is calculated if you’re doing that.”
Billy:  “Awesome!  I attack!”
David:  “Since this is a versus roll, and you are a player, the 
NPC’s roll is determined first, so I’ll roll it’s Dodge.”  
David looks at the stats to his monster.  His monster has 3D10 in 
Dexterity and a Dodge skill of 4.  He rolls the dice and gets a 7, 8, 
and 9.  His top two dice are 8 and 9, adding a Dodge of 4 gives 
the creature a Defense Score of 21.
David: “You have to get 21 or higher to hit it.”
Billy rolls his Dexterity plus Melee, then applies the To-Hit modifier 
of his knife.   His 2D8 Dexterity dice roll a 2 and an 8.  Since the 
D8 exploded, he gets an additional 1D10 to his dice pool.  The 
D10 rolls another 8.  His top two dice add up to 16, he has a 2 in 
melee, and his small knife has a +5 To-Hit.
Billy:  “I got a 23!!  I spend all three of my Exertion to in-
crease my damage!”
Billy has a Combat Rating of 3, but he spends 3 Exertion to bring 
this value to a 6.  Momentum rules state that for each unit traveled 
before a melee attack, you increase a Character’s Combat Rating 
by one, but the bonus cannot exceed the Characters current Com-
bat Rating.  Since Billy’s current Combat Rating is 6, and he moved 
4 Units, he gets the full bonus, making his Combat Rating a 10 for 
this attack.
David: “Can I see the stats for that knife again?”
David: “Okay!  You definitely stab the thing!  You have a 
Base Damage of 1D4, and an X Damage of 1D4.  X Damage 
is multiplied by your Combat Rating, which is currently 10. 
This means you deal a total of 11D4 damage!
Billy:  “Yeah!  Take that!”
Billy rolls 11D4 and adds them all together, totaling 26 damage 
to the creature.
Billy:  “26 damage!”
David: “That’s a lot of damage!  But...not enough to take this 
thing down.  Okay Billy, you get to decide who acts next.”
Billy:  “Charles!”
Charles:  “Well, if I see all this going down, I’m going to try 
to grab the laptop and run over to the door next to Isabella.”
Billy:  “No!  Pull out your gun and help me kill this thing!”
Charles:  “Ehhh, Okay fine, it does look injured, correct?”

Billy:  “Two.”

David: “Well, maybe not.  Time to roll for a Mutilation!”
The first time a Character goes below zero HP each combat in-
stance, a Mutilation is applied to the attack which caused the dam-
age.  David declares that the creature has a cutting type of attack, 
and rolls on the Mutilation chart on page 67.  You always roll 
1D12 to determine a Mutilation.  David rolls an 11.
See “Limbloss” on page 69.  Limbloss means that the attack 
deals one quarter damage, which is applied to your Max HP in-
stead of current, but also a roll is made to see what limb is lost. 
David rolls 1D6 and gets a 1.
David: “You don’t take 24 damage, you only take 6.  But it 
gets worse.”
Billy:  “I have 9 HP left.  What’s the bad news?”
David: “Actually, you still have 15 hit points left!  The 6 ‘dam-
age’ is instead applied to your maximum hit points!”
Billy:  “Oh no!  Is that permanent?”
David: “Yes, also everyone sees blood spray from Adams 
left side.  As the creature makes it’s second attack, Adams left 
arm is cut clean off!!”

Billy rolls 3D6 for Willpower and scores a 12.

take any actions on your turn you also take 1D4 damage. 
Also you take one damage every minute until you are at full 
hp again.  This concludes the first combat round!”

ture acted last on the second combat round, it gets to decide

ture is leaving a unit adjacent to you.  This means you get an 
attack of opportunity.  If you want, you can make a free melee 
attack as it flees from you.”
Billy:  “I might as well try!”
Attacks of opportunity ignore who’s Turn it is, and who has and 
hasn’t acted.  They are independent versus rolls of Dexterity plus 
Melee versus Dexterity plus Dodge.  The Director determines the 
creatures Defense Score.  It has 3D10 Dexterity dice and a Dodge 
skill of 4.  It rolls 4, 5, and 8.
David: “The creature rolled a 17.”
Billy rolls his 2D8 Dexterity dice and gets a 6 and a 1.  His melee 
skill is 2, and his knife gives a plus 5 bonus.

in ritual room Alpha-3.  I can hear SCP-2845 just north of my 
location.  If you can gain access to the doorways and create a 
path to the center chamber I can lure it into position.  Over.”
Charles:  “How does this guy know me?!”
David: “A lot can happen in two years.  His voice doesn’t 
sound familiar, though.”
Charles:  “I see if I can open the doorways from ritual room

ment chambers, then I ask Emily if she can see anything on

(Charles) Norton: “Emily, can you find ritual room Alpha-3?

(David) Emily:  “The cameras are a lil’ static-y, I can’t see

(Charles) Norton: “That’s where he said he was.”
David: “First you successfully open the door to the south of 
ritual room Alpha-3.”
(David) Emily:  “I just saw a door open in the room.”
(Charles) Norton: “Good, that’s the right room then.  K-10, 
did the door in your room open?”
(David) K-10:  “10-4 Norton!  Access to the inner ritual room 
is now accessible.  Open the door to the north now and I’ll 
begin to lead SCP-2845 into the central chamber.  Hurry! 
There’s no time this thing is roaming too close to the exit!”
Charles:  “I open the door to the north!  Then I start working

David requests a few more Computer skill checks, and the doors

(David) Emily:  “I see it!  The deer creature!  It’s going into 
the Alpha-3 room!  I still don’t see K-10 though.  What is he,

David: “Going back to Isabella and Adam, the guard is

(Amanda) Isabella:  “What are you doing here?!”

(Amanda) Isabella:  “This is the US Military cybernetics spe-

Amanda:  “I point to my cybernetic eye.”

bernetic specialist?”

David asks Charles if he would meet him in the other room.  The

Part 10: The Z Machine
Amanda:  “I got a 12”
Billy: “I got a 3.”
David: “As you both run to the central chamber area, you 
hear a loud crash behind you.  As you’re running you peak 
behind you and see black shape standing on four legs.  It’s 
mouth opens wide and a massive fireball fires at Adam!”
Billy: “Nooo!”
David: “Roll your Dexterity plus Dodge, but I’m giving you 
a -2 penalty since it’s sort of an ambush attack from behind. 
The creature rolled an 18 to hit you.”
Billy: “I got a 7.”
David: “The fireball roars down the hallway with black 
smoke trailing behind.  Take 23 damage, and you’re on fire!”
Billy: “My max hit points are only 22 now!  I’m dead!”
David: “Well, let me roll for a Fire Mutilation and we’ll see”
David rolls 1D12 and gets a 5.  He consults the Mutilation list for 
Fire damage and determines that Billy gets “Disfigured.”
The Mutilation reads:
The attack deals half damage (which turns 23 damage into 11 be-
cause it’s rounded down.)  Your Character now has a permanent 
disfigurement on their face or upper area of the body.  Director 
decides the specifics of the disfigurement.  Your Charisma dice no 
longer “explode”.  This Effect is Permanent.
Billy: “I’m hideous!  I scream...”
(Billy) Adam: “My face!  My face this time!”
David: “Okay, the creature uses it’s second action to fire an-
other fireball.  It’s shooting you again Billy!”
Billy: “Why do you want me dead!?”
David: “The creature rolls an 18 to-hit.”
Billy: “I got a 15 to dodge, but I only have 11 hit points left!”
David: “This time you take 13 damage, but this creature has 
a special attack where each hit increases your Fire Intensity 
level by three.  You were attacked twice, so you’re Fire Inten-
sity level gets +6.  Characters can only have up to a maximum 
of 5 for their Fire Intensity, so you’re at max.  At the end of this 
round you’re going to take another 5D10 damage!”
Billy: “I’m already at negative two!  I’m so dead!”
David: “Not yet.  The creature passes the turn to Dr. Wiley. 
He takes off his lab coat and tries to put out the flames.  Your 
fire intensity level is thus reduced by one.  That’s his turn.  Dr. 
Wiley passes the turn to Amanda.”
Amanda:  “Aim, Fire!”
Amanda’s gun has a To-Hit modifier of 1, and an Aim of 5.  David 
determines that the fire beast is only 6 Units behind them.  Her gun 
has a range of 4, so she’ll get a -1 to hit.  She rolls her Dexterity + 
Firearms (which is 2x2) but also decides to spend two Exertion to 
add +2D12 to her To-Hit roll.  She ends up scoring a 22.  The fire 
beast has a Reaction score of 10.  It doesn’t get to dodge since it’s 
already acted.  Critical hit!  She rolls 3D8 for damage and it adds 
up to 20!  The creature then must roll it’s Health + Poise difficulty 20 
to resist stagger, but it fails.  Amanda passes the turn to Billy.
David: “Okay Billy, you’re at negative 2.  Roll your Fate plus 
Resist Death difficulty 4.”
Billy: “I can’t fail that.  My Fate rating is 2, and I have a 2 for 
Resist Death, so I automatically succeed.”
David: “Okay, you’re alive for now.  You’re at zero hit points 
though, and you only get one action.  Your fire intensity lev-
el is currently at 4 thanks to the doctor.  Since you went be-
low zero hit points, you’re automatically in the prone posi-
tion.  If you end your turn still in the prone position, your Fire

Intensity level will be reduced by 3.  Since you had to roll a

ficulty 7 and I’ll let Adam roll around on the ground a bit, but

Billy succeeds the skill check.
David: “I’ll reduce your Fire Intensity to 2, and at the end of

Billy rolls 2D10 and takes 6 more damage.

David: “Okay, but since you’re at negative six, at the start of

Billy: “I’m spending all three Exertion on this roll.”

His total is 17.
David: “You’re back at zero!  What do you do?  You only get

fore.  It is immune to Mutilations, and therefore you cannot

Amanda:  “Okay I’ll do that then, I guess.”
The creature has a Stagger score of 6.  Amanda needs a Critical

Amanda:  “Got em!  Now I’m spending my last point of Ex-
ertion to increase my Projectile rating.”
Amanda calculates her final damage as being 4D8, but adds an

Amanda:  “Take 48 damage, hot head!”

out a med kit once on a character before they need a short rest.”

(Amanda) Isabella:  “That’s not what we need to hear right

(Charles) Norton: “I’m sorry but one of you is going to need

(Charles) Norton: “The doctor?”
(Amanda) Isabella:  “Nevermind, just tell me what I need to do.”
(Charles) Norton: “We need the Z Machine primed first.  It’s

(Amanda) Isabella:  “We’ve got that part covered, what’s

(Amanda) Isabella:  “I get it.  Everything in the room will die.

David: “You hear the central containment room come to life. 
An electrical hum gets louder and louder.  
(David) K-10:  “Now is the time Norton!  Open the doors!”
(Charles) Norton: “She’s not in position!”
(David) K-10:  “It’s too late!  Open these doors immediately

Charles:  “I suppose I don’t have a choice!  I open both the 
northern and southern entrances!”
(Charles) Norton: “Close your eyes!”
Amanda:  “I activate my heat vision!”
David: “Billy, roll your Perception plus Awareness.”
Billy: “I got a 15.”

David: “Amanda, you see a deer shaped figure walking into 
the central containment area.  Your heat vision makes details

normal sized deer.  It seems to be following a smaller crea-
ture.  Billy, the doors opened up so fast you couldn’t help but 
notice the Deer God standing at the other side of the room.

David: “This roll isn’t to see if you recall the creature.  It’s to

Part 11: Epilogue
David: “Two weeks later there is a ceremony to honor the 
dead back at Site-112.  Foster constructs a monument dedi-
cated to the memory of the most valued D-Class to ever serve 
the SCP Foundation.  The monument is a brass, one armed 
man in a D-Class outfit which was erected at the front of the 
facility.  SCP-1316 is given permission to roam Site-112 freely, 
and has increased morale throughout the facility.  Norton is 
promoted to MTF Director.  Dr. Wiley is hired on as a cyber-
netics specialist.  Emily Stern maintains her role as Mainte-
nance Director but now at Site-112.  Isabella...wakes up.”
Amanda:  “Huh?”
David: “The destruction of SCP-2845 reverted all those con-
verted into pillars back into human form!  You see Dr. Wiley 
standing over you.
(David) Dr. Wiley  “It appears the effects of being converted 
into a pillar of biological rubber and then back again has had 
some regenerative properties I’d like to study, Mrs. IronSight. 
Or perhaps that’s no longer an appropriate title?”
David: “You feel different.  Then it hits you.  Your eye.  It’s no 
longer cybernetic!”
(Amanda) Isabella:  “What happened?”
David: “The doctor catches you up with current events.  It 
turns out that shape-shifting back and forth from the pillar 
back into human form has somehow restored your eye...!  
I think that concludes this adventure!  If you would all like to 
end the campaign here, this is a great place to stop.”
Charles:  “No way!  Let’s keep playing!”
Amanda:  “Yeah I want to see what happens from here!”
Billy: “I wouldn’t mind making another character.  Maybe I 
can use that advanced character creation this time!”
David: “Well if that’s the case, Amanda, get rid of your Cy-
bernetic Eye Aspect.  Everyone gets two end of Chapter Re-
wards.  Billy, I’ll help you make a new character next week. 
Do you mind if I suggest an Aspect for your next character?”
Billy: “Sure!  Which one?  The one that gives me more hit

David: “Actually I was thinking of the one where you can 
play as a cat.”
Billy: “You mean...!!!”
David: “Yes, you can play as K-10 if you want!”
Billy: “Hell yeah!!”

Glossary
Strength
Strength (STR) is a measure of physical power and raw mus-
cle capability.  It represents a character’s physical might, raw 
force, and ability to exert physical dominance.  Characters 
with a STR rating of 2 would be of below average strength and 
appear thin or weak, while STR rating of 16 would appear as 
a bodybuilder.  High STR helps increase your Combat Multipli-
er, which increases your close quarters combat damage.
Dexterity
Dexterity (DEX) is a measure of agility, finesse, nimbleness, 
and precision in physical tasks.  A DEX rating of 2 would rep-
resent someone who is clumsy, while a rating of 16 represents 
a character with cat-like balance and ninja-like reflexes.  High 
DEX increases your Movement speed.
Perception
Perception (PER) is the ability to observe and interpret sen-
sory information from the environment, as well as hand-eye 
coordination.  It encompasses a character’s awareness, alert-
ness, and ability to notice details and detect subtle cues.  PER 
ratings of 2 would represent an overtly oblivious individual, 
while 16 would be someone with high precision and obser-
vational skills.  High PER increases your Projectile Multiplier, 
which increases your damage with firearms.
Health
Health (HTH) is a measure of a character’s physical well-be-
ing and resilience.  It represents their overall vitality, endur-
ance, and ability to withstand and recover from damage or 
injuries.  A HTH rating of 2 would represent an unhealthy 
character that doesn’t take care of themselves, while a rating 
of 16 would be a character that eats right and exercises daily.
Physical Attributes
Attributes
Intellect
Intellect (INT) is a measure of a character’s mental capacity, 
knowledge, and reasoning ability.  It encompasses their analyt-
ical thinking, problem-solving skills, memory, and general intel-
lectual capabilities.  An INT rating of 2 would represent a child, 
or simple minded individual, while a 16 would be a genius.
Willpower
Willpower (WIL) is the mental strength and determination of 
a Character to resist or overcome challenges, temptations, or 
mental fatigue.  It represents their ability to focus, maintain 
concentration, and push through difficult situations.  Willpow-
er can also be associated with the surge of adrenaline or en-
ergy that fuels a character’s resolve in critical moments.  A 
WIL rating of 2 would be a person whom often succumbs to 
desires and random impulses, while a rating of 16 would be a 
character who has total control over their emotions and fears.
Charisma
Charisma (CHR) is the natural charm, persuasion, and mag-
netism a character possesses.  It reflects their ability to influ-
ence and interact with others, to make a favorable impres-
sion, friends, and to effectively communicate their ideas and 
intentions.  Charisma is often a key factor in leadership, nego-
tiation, and social interactions, allowing a character to sway 
opinions and gain the trust and admiration of others.  Charac-
ters with a CHR rating of 2 would be socially awkward while 
a rating of 16 would be the life of every party.
Fate
Fate (FTE) is the mysterious force that governs the twists and 
turns of destiny.  It represents a character’s luck, fortune, 
and ability to defy the odds.  The attribute of Fate influences 
outcomes that are beyond one’s control and determines the 
likelihood of favorable or unfavorable events.  It also plays a 
crucial role in determining a character’s resilience and surviv-
al in dire situations, as it influences their ability to cheat death 
or overcome seemingly impossible odds.  Characters with a 
FTE rating of 2 would feel that they were unlucky, while a 
rating of 16 would feel like they have a guardian spirit of for-
tune helping them along the way.  When the Director needs a 
Character to make a “luck” roll, they can request a Fate check 
to be made without any skills added.
Mental Attributes
Glossary
182


Crucial Skills
Standard Skills
Skills
Awareness
The Awareness skill is governed by Perception.  When spending 
Skill Points, your Awareness skill cannot exceed half of your Per-
ception Rating. Awareness is a Crucial Skill which means that it has 
a somewhat higher importance than most other skills.
Awareness is a skill representing a character’s ability to ob-
serve and perceive their surroundings, detect hidden or sub-
tle details, and notice potential threats or opportunities.  It 
encompasses sharp senses, situational awareness, and the 
ability to gather information through observation.
Dodge
The Dodge skill is governed by Dexterity.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Dodge skill cannot exceed half of your Dexterity Rat-
ing. Dodge is a Crucial Skill which means that it has a somewhat 
higher importance than most other skills.
Dodge is a skill representing a character’s agility and reflexes 
to evade incoming attacks or hazards.  It involves quick move-
ments, nimbleness, and the ability to anticipate and react to 
danger swiftly.  A higher Dodge skill increases the chance of 
successfully avoiding harm or mitigating the impact of attacks, 
both actively (by Dodging) and passively (Reaction score).
Firearms
The Firearms skill is governed by Perception.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Firearms skill cannot exceed half of your Perception 
Rating. Firearms is a Crucial Skill which means that it has a some-
what higher importance than most other skills.
Firearms is a skill encompassing the knowledge and proficien-
cy in handling and using various types of firearms.  It includes 
the understanding of firearm mechanics, aiming, shooting 
techniques, reloading, and maintaining weapons.  A higher 
Firearms skill improves accuracy, control, and effectiveness 
when wielding firearms in combat or other situations requiring 
their use.
Melee
The Melee skill is governed by Dexterity.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Melee skill cannot exceed half of your Dexterity Rat-
ing. Melee is a Crucial Skill which means that it has a somewhat 
higher importance than most other skills.
Melee is a skill representing proficiency in close-quarters 
combat and the use of various melee weapons.  It covers tech-
niques such as striking, parrying, and maneuvering in combat 
with weapons like swords, knives, clubs, or unarmed strikes. 
A higher Melee skill enhances accuracy and overall effective-
ness when engaging in melee combat.
Animal Ken
The Animal Ken skill is governed by Charisma.  When spending 
Skill Points, your Animal Ken skill cannot exceed half of your Cha-
risma Rating.
Animal Ken is a skill of understanding and interacting with 
animals.  It encompasses the ability to communicate with, 
handle, train, and influence wildlife.  With the Animal Ken 
skill, characters can establish rapport, calm agitated crea-
tures, give commands, and utilize their knowledge of animal 
behavior to their advantage.
Anomalistics
The Anomalistics skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Anomalistics skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Anomalistics is the skill of studying and comprehending anom-
alies within the SCP universe.  It involves deep knowledge of 
specific SCPs, their anomalous properties, and the underlying 
principles behind them.  Characters with the Anomalistics skill 
are well-versed in identifying, analyzing, and understanding 
the paranormal phenomena encountered in the SCP universe. 
They can decipher the nature of anomalies, recognize their ef-
fects, and develop strategies to interact with or counteract them.
Athletics
The Athletics skill is governed by Health.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Athletics skill cannot exceed half of your Health Rating. 
Athletics is a skill of physical prowess and movement abilities. 
Characters with the Athletics skill excel in physical activities 
such as running, jumping, climbing, and swimming.  They 
possess enhanced strength, agility, and endurance, allow-
ing them to perform feats of athleticism with ease.  This skill 
encompasses activities requiring physical exertion, coordina-
tion, and overall athletic performance.  Characters with high 
Athletics will excel in actions like sprinting, acrobatics, bal-
ancing, sports, and other physical challenges.
Brawn
The Brawn skill is governed by Strength.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Brawn skill cannot exceed half of your Strength Rating.
Brawn is a skill representing raw physical power and strength. 
Characters with high Brawn possess exceptional muscular 
strength and the ability to exert force.  This skill allows charac-
ters to perform feats of strength, such as lifting heavy objects, 
breaking through barriers, and overpowering opponents in 
physical confrontations.  Brawn is a measure of a character’s 
physical might and their capacity for brute force.
Glossary
183


Break Free
The Break Free skill is governed by Strength.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Break Free skill cannot exceed half of your Strength Rating.
The Break Free skill represents the ability to escape from var-
ious forms of confinement, restraints, or traps.  Characters 
with high Break Free skill possess the dexterity and knowl-
edge necessary to disable restraints, and find ways to free 
themselves from imprisonment or being physically detained.
Computers
The Computers skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Computers skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating.
The Computers skill represents a proficiency in operating and 
utilizing computer systems, software, and digital devices. 
Characters with a high Computers skill possess knowledge 
of computer hardware, software applications, and general 
computer usage.  They can navigate user interfaces, perform 
basic troubleshooting, access and manipulate files, and uti-
lize common productivity tools.  This skill allows characters to 
interact with digital environments, perform tasks such as data 
retrieval and analysis, and effectively use technology to their 
advantage.
Creativity
The Creativity skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Creativity skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Creativity represents a character’s ability for artistic expres-
sion and creative endeavors.  This skill combines various cre-
ative disciplines such as writing, playing musical instruments, 
singing, visual arts, poetry, and other forms of artistic expres-
sion.  Characters with high Creativity possess a natural talent 
for expressing themselves through their chosen medium, be 
it words, music, or visual arts. They can compose melodies, 
write compelling stories, create captivating artworks, and 
convey emotions through their artistic creations.
Deceive
The Deceive skill is governed by Charisma.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Deceive skill cannot exceed half of your Charisma Rating. 
Deceive is the skill of manipulating others through lies, persua-
sion, and trickery.  Characters with high Deceive skill excel at 
convincing others to believe falsehoods, misleading them with 
carefully crafted words, and creating false impressions.  They 
can skillfully twist the truth, exploit psychological vulnerabili-
ties, and fabricate convincing stories to achieve their goals.
Disarm
The Disarm skill is governed by Dexterity.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Disarm skill cannot exceed half of your Dexterity Rating.
Disarm is the art of effectively disarming opponents or ren-
dering their weapons useless.  Characters who excel in the 
Disarm skill possess the ability to quickly analyze and exploit 
weaknesses in how their adversaries handle weapons en-
abling them to disarm opponents with precision and finesse. 
They can anticipate weapon movements, take advantage of 
openings and utilize techniques to separate adversaries from 
their weapons or temporarily disable them.  When facing 
armed foes as it allows characters to strategically neutralize 
their opponents weapons and gain a tactical edge.  A high 
level of expertise in Disarm allows characters to swiftly neu-
tralize threats and transform conflicts into manageable cir-
cumstances.
Disguise
The Disguise skill is governed by Charisma.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Disguise skill cannot exceed half of your Charisma Rating. 
Disguise is skill of altering one’s appearance, demeanor, and 
behavior to assume a different identity or blend into a specif-
ic role or environment.  Characters with a high Disguise skill 
possess the ability to transform their appearance through the 
use of makeup, costumes, props, and acting techniques.  They 
can mimic different voices, gestures, and mannerisms to con-
vincingly portray a different persona.
Intimidate
The Intimidate skill is governed by Charisma.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Intimidate skill cannot exceed half of your Charisma Rating. 
A skill of instilling fear, exerting dominance, or using threats 
and coercion to manipulate or control others.  Characters 
with a high Intimidate skill possess a commanding presence, 
imposing physicality, or a knack for psychological manipula-
tion that allows them to intimidate and influence those around 
them.  They can employ a combination of body language, 
vocal tone, and verbal threats to intimidate targets, forcing 
compliance, extracting information, or deterring potential 
adversaries.  In some situations the characters SUS rating is 
added to Intimidate skill checks (Director’s discretion).
Glossary
184


Intuition
The Intuition skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Intuition skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
The skill of perceiving subtle cues, sensing hidden dangers, or 
having an innate understanding of the situation.  Characters 
with a high Intuition possess a heightened sense of awareness 
and are adept at picking up on nonverbal signals, environ-
mental changes, or supernatural phenomena that may elude 
others. This skill allows Characters to trust their instincts, make 
intuitive leaps, and sense the presence of something out of the 
ordinary.  It can be used to detect hidden motives, uncover 
hidden objects or passages, or anticipate imminent dangers. 
Intuition grants characters almost a sixth sense that helps them 
in uncertain situations. It can provide valuable insights, warn 
of impending threats, or guide characters towards the right 
path when faced with uncertainty.
Investigate
The Investigate skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Investigate skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Investigate is the skill of gathering information, analyzing 
clues, and conducting thorough investigations.  Characters 
with a high Investigate are skilled at conducting research, 
searching for evidence, and piecing together information to 
uncover hidden truths.  This skill allows them to ask the right 
questions, examine crime scenes, interview witnesses, and 
follow leads to unravel mysteries or solve complex problems. 
With their keen eye for detail and logical thinking, characters 
proficient in Investigate can uncover hidden connections, de-
cipher codes, and uncover hidden motives.  Investigate is an 
essential skill for detectives, researchers, and anyone seeking 
to uncover the truth behind enigmatic occurrences or puzzling 
phenomena.
Mathematics
The Mathematics skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Mathematics skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Mathematics is a skill for numerical calculations, prob-
lem-solving, and logical reasoning.  Proficiency in Mathemat-
ics enables complex calculations, estimating probabilities, 
and making accurate deductions.  Apply mathematical princi-
ples to analyze data, predict outcomes, and identify patterns. 
Useful in engineering, cryptography, and scientific research. 
Enables the understanding and manipulation of mathematical 
concepts, formulas, finance, and equations.
Mechanics
The Mechanics skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill Points, 
your Mechanics skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Mechanics is the skill for understanding and working with 
mechanical systems, devices, and machinery.  A high level in 
Mechanics allows for repairing, maintaining, and construct-
ing mechanical objects.  Knowledge of mechanical principles, 
gears, levers, and engines.  Ability to troubleshoot and diag-
nose mechanical issues.
Medical
The Medical skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Medical skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
The Medical skill encompasses knowledge and expertise in 
providing medical care and treatment.  Proficiency in Medical 
allows for assessing injuries and illnesses, administering first 
aid, performing medical procedures, and providing ongoing 
medical care.  Knowledge of anatomy, physiology, pharma-
cology, and medical techniques.
If your Character is considered a Medical Professional, then ad-
ditional options become available with the Medical Skill such as 
surgery, endoscopy, intravenous anesthesia/amnestic administra-
tion, advanced therapy, etc.
Navigate
The Navigate skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Navigate skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Navigate involves the ability to find one’s way and traverse 
unfamiliar terrains.  A high Navigate skill allows for reading 
maps, using GPS, triangulating positions, and utilizing land-
marks to determine direction and navigate through various 
environments.  It encompasses knowledge of geography, 
spatial orientation, and navigation tools.
Ordnance
The Ordnance skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Ordnance skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Ordnance is a skill encompassing the knowledge and han-
dling of explosives, heavy weaponry, and military-grade 
armaments.  A high Ordnance skill allows for safe handling, 
loading, and firing of explosives, rocket launchers, mounted 
weapons, and other advanced destructive tools.  It involves 
understanding explosive properties, munition types, prop-
er deployment techniques, and safety protocols.  Utilize the 
Ordnance skill to effectively handle heavy weapons, employ 
demolitions, and engage in tactical combat situations involv-
ing high-powered ordnance.
Glossary
185


Persuade
The Persuade skill is governed by Charisma.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Persuade skill cannot exceed half of your Charisma Rating. 
Persuade is a skill used to effectively influence and convince 
others through communication, charisma, and logical reason-
ing.  It involves the ability to articulate ideas, sway opinions, 
negotiate, and manipulate social situations to achieve desired 
outcomes.  Characters with a high Persuade give convincing 
arguments and possess the ability to navigate delicate or 
challenging conversations. Apply Persuade to negotiate 
deals, win over skeptical individuals, manipulate emotions, 
or gather information through conversation.
Poise
The Poise skill is governed by Health.  When spending Skill Points, 
your Poise skill cannot exceed half of your Health Rating. 
The Poise skill represents a character’s sense of balance and 
stability.  It is utilized when maintaining equilibrium, resisting 
falling or stumbling, and remaining steady in precarious situ-
ations.  Poise is crucial in scenarios such as walking on narrow 
ledges, traversing unstable terrain, or withstanding external 
forces that could knock one off balance. A high level of Poise 
enables characters to maintain composure and avoid physical 
mishaps that may result in falling or loss of control (Stagger).
Psychology
The Psychology skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Psychology skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Psychology is the skill that encompasses the understanding of 
human behavior, emotions, and thought processes.  It allows 
characters to analyze and interpret the motivations, reac-
tions, emotions, and mental states of others.  With this skill, 
characters can assess someone’s intentions, detect deception, 
and gain insights into their psyche.
Resilience
The Resilience skill is governed by Willpower.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Resilience skill cannot exceed half of your Willpower Rating. 
This skill represents the character’s ability to endure physical 
harm, withstand poisons, and resist sickness.  It encompasses 
the capacity to withstand and recover from physical and bio-
logical stresses on the body.  Characters with a high Resilience 
are more resistant to injuries, toxins, diseases, sickness, and 
other harmful effects.
Resist Death
The Resist Death skill is governed by Fate.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Resist Death skill cannot exceed half of your Fate Rating. 
Resist Death represents the character’s ability to defy mortal-
ity and resist the brink of death.  It encompasses the fortitude 
to withstand fatal injuries, deadly conditions, and near-death 
experiences.  Characters with high Resist Death have a higher 
chance of surviving life-threatening situations and recovering 
from severe injuries.
Self-Control
The Self-Control skill is governed by Willpower.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Self-Control skill cannot exceed half of your Willpower Rating. 
Self-Control is the character’s discipline and willpower in 
maintaining composure and controlling their emotions and 
impulses.  It encompasses the ability to resist temptations, stay 
focused under pressure, and maintain mental and emotion-
al stability in challenging situations.  Characters with a high 
Self-Control exhibit better self-regulation, making them less 
susceptible to manipulation, intimidation, terrorization, and 
emotional outbursts.
Stealth
The Stealth skill is governed by Dexterity.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Stealth skill cannot exceed half of your Dexterity Rating. 
Stealth is the character’s ability to move silently, remain un-
noticed, and blend into their surroundings.  It involves tech-
niques such as avoiding detection, hiding in shadows, moving 
silently, and maintaining a low profile.  Characters with high 
Stealth are skilled at staying concealed, infiltrating secure ar-
eas undetected, and avoiding potential threats.  This skill is 
essential for covert operations, espionage, and ambushing 
enemies without being detected.  It encompasses aspects of 
agility, awareness of one’s surroundings, and the ability to 
minimize noise and visual cues that may give away the Char-
acter’s presence.
Teaching
The Teaching skill is governed by Charisma.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Teaching skill cannot exceed half of your Charisma Rating. 
Teaching is the skill for a Character’s ability to impart knowl-
edge and instruct others effectively.  It involves the capacity to 
communicate information clearly, engage students, and adapt 
teaching methods to suit different individuals or groups.  Char-
acters with high Teaching are adept at conveying complex 
concepts, breaking down information into manageable parts, 
and fostering a positive and conducive learning environment. 
Glossary
186


Technology
The Technology skill is governed by Intellect.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Technology skill cannot exceed half of your Intellect Rating. 
Technology is the skill for working with modern gadgets, de-
vices, and advanced technological systems.  It encompasses 
knowledge of electronics, hardware, and other technological 
aspects.  Characters with high Technology are skilled at trou-
bleshooting, repairing, and backwards engineering complex 
machinery and equipment.  They possess a deep understand-
ing of how technology works and can adapt to new advance-
ments and innovations.
Thievery
The Thievery skill is governed by Dexterity.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Thievery skill cannot exceed half of your Dexterity Rating. 
This skill represents the character’s aptitude for stealthy ac-
tions, sleight of hand, and illicit activities.  It encompasses the 
ability to pilfer items unnoticed, perform acts of larceny and 
subterfuge, and navigate through environments without de-
tection.  Characters with high Thievery possess nimble fingers, 
a keen eye for valuable objects, and knowledge of the tricks 
of the trade.  They are skilled in bypassing security measures 
and lock-picking.
Vehicles
The Vehicles skill is governed by Dexterity.  When spending Skill 
Points, your Vehicles skill cannot exceed half of your Dexterity Rating. 
Vehicles is a skill that represents the character’s proficiency in 
operating and maneuvering various types of vehicles.  It en-
compasses the knowledge of driving, piloting, and controlling 
vehicles such as cars, motorcycles, boats, aircraft, and more. 
Characters with high Vehicles skill possess a deep understand-
ing of vehicle mechanics, navigation, and handling.  They can 
effectively steer through challenging terrain, perform evasive 
maneuvers, and operate vehicles with precision.
Characters with a Vehicles bonus of 4 or more are qualified to 
operate air vehicles such as airplanes and helicopters.  Vehicle 
bonuses of 6 or more allow you to operate military vehicles such 
as fighter jets and tanks. 
Architecture
The Architecture skill does not have a governing Attribute, and 
therefore does not have a skill cap.
Characters with the Architecture skill excel in the design, plan-
ning, and construction of buildings and structures.  Charac-
ters with architecture skills possess knowledge of architectural 
principles, styles, and techniques.  They understand how to 
create functional and aesthetically pleasing spaces, consider 
structural integrity and safety, and utilize materials effectively. 
This skill involves the ability to read and create architectural 
drawings, blueprints, and schematics, as well as understand-
ing the spatial relationships and proportions within a building.
Characters without the Architecture skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a general Intellect check, however the difficulty is doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Engineering Aspect on page 44.
Astronomy
The Astronomy skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.
Proficiency in the study and observation of celestial objects, 
including stars, planets, galaxies, and other cosmic phenom-
ena.  Characters with astronomy skills possess knowledge 
about the structure of the universe, astronomical theories, and 
the principles governing celestial bodies.  This skill involves the 
ability to identify and interpret constellations, track the move-
ments of planets and stars, and understand the fundamentals 
of astrophysics.  Characters with astronomy skills can calcu-
late celestial coordinates, predict astronomical events such as 
eclipses or meteor showers, and analyze astronomical data to 
draw conclusions about the nature of the universe.  This skill is 
particularly useful for Characters involved in space explora-
tion, navigation, or research related to celestial phenomena.
Characters without the Astronomy skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a general Intellect check, however the difficulty is doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Ecology (page 44) or As-
tronaut (page 46) Aspects.
Chemistry
The Chemistry skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.
The Chemistry skill represents an understanding and knowl-
edge of the properties, composition, and interactions of 
substances.  Characters with chemistry skills are adept at 
studying and manipulating chemicals, conducting exper-
iments, and analyzing reactions.  They possess a deep un-
derstanding of the periodic table, molecular structures, 
and various chemical processes.  Chemistry skills can be 
applied to tasks such as synthesizing compounds, identi-
fying unknown substances, analyzing the effects of chemi-
cals, and creating chemical reactions for specific purposes. 
Characters without the Chemistry skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a Medical check, however the difficulty is doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Chemistry Aspect on page 44.
Written-In Skills
Glossary
187


Cooking
The Cooking skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.
The ability to prepare and create delicious and nourishing 
meals.  Characters with high cooking skills have knowledge of 
various culinary techniques, ingredients, and cooking meth-
ods.  They can follow recipes, improvise dishes, and under-
stand the art of flavor combinations.  Cooking skills include 
(culinary) knife skills, food preparation, seasoning, cooking 
temperatures, and presentation.  Characters proficient in 
cooking can create meals that satisfy hunger, provide heal-
ing benefits, and even impress others with their culinary cre-
ations.  They may have expertise in specific cuisines or dietary 
preferences, such as baking, grilling, vegetarian cooking, or 
gourmet cuisine.
Characters without the Cooking skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a Creativity check, however the food quality will be above 
average at best.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Culinary Arts Aspect on page 44.
Detect Lie
The Detect Lie skill has a Skill Cap equal to your Character’s 
Psychology Skill.
Detect Lie is a skill that represents the ability to detect when 
someone is withholding information, altering the truth, or ly-
ing about something.  It involves keen perception of subtle 
facial movements, body language, and deceptive wordplay. 
Only Characters that have the Detect Lie skill are capable of 
truly utilizing this difficult to master ability.  Directors may roll 
the Detect Lie skill for the player to help obfuscate the results, 
or give unknown difficulties.
Characters without the Detect Lie skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a Psychology check, however the Director should increase the 
difficulty by 1D12 and provide vague results at best.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Detect Lies Aspect on page 49.
Engineering
The Engineering skill does not have a governing Attribute, and 
therefore does not have a skill cap.
Proficiency in applying scientific and mathematical princi-
ples to design, develop, and construct practical solutions to 
various problems.  Characters with engineering skills pos-
sess knowledge of various engineering disciplines, such as 
mechanical, electrical, civil, or aerospace engineering, and 
can use their expertise to analyze, design, and optimize sys-
tems, structures, and devices.  They understand the principles 
of mechanics, thermodynamics, materials science, and other 
relevant fields to create innovative solutions and improve ex-
isting technologies, or invent new ones.  The Engineering skill 
allows Characters to design and build complex machinery, 
create efficient and sustainable infrastructure, and solve tech-
nical challenges.  This skill is essential for Characters involved 
in invention, construction, research, development, and tech-
nological advancements.  Characters with engineering skills 
have the ability to push the boundaries of what is possible and 
explore new frontiers of technology. (continued)
Characters without the Engineering skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a Mechanics check, however the difficulty is doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Engineering Aspect on page 44.
Fighting Style
The Fighting Style skill does not have a governing Attribute, and 
therefore does not have a skill cap.  This skills name is not written in 
as Fighting Style, and instead is chosen by the player.
When choosing a Fighting Style, you can name it anything 
you desire, real or fictional.  Good examples include Capoei-
ra, Ninjutsu, Systema, Gun Kata, Hado, or Drunken Fighting. 
The skill can replace any Melee skill check.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Unique Fighting Style Aspect 
on page 50.
Hacking
The Hacking skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.
The skill of gaining unauthorized access to computer systems, 
networks, and electronic devices.  Characters with a high 
Hacking skill possess expertise in computer programming, 
network security, and exploiting vulnerabilities in digital sys-
tems.  They can bypass security measures, crack passwords, 
retrieve sensitive information, and manipulate digital data. 
Hacking skills are valuable for infiltrating secure systems, con-
ducting cyber espionage, protecting against digital threats, 
and navigating virtual environments.
Characters without the Hacking skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a Computers check, however the difficulty should be doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Computer Programmer Aspect 
on page 44.
History
The History skill does not have a governing Attribute, and therefore 
does not have a skill cap.
The understanding and knowledge of past events, civiliza-
tions, cultures, and significant milestones in human history. 
Characters with history skills possess a wide range of infor-
mation about historical periods, important figures, major 
conflicts, and societal developments.  They can interpret his-
torical texts, analyze artifacts, and piece together historical 
narratives.  History skills allow characters to uncover hidden 
secrets, decipher ancient writings, and gain insights into the 
motivations and actions of past societies.
Characters without the History skill may still attempt these tasks with 
a general Intellect check however the information provided will be 
superficial at best.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Historian Aspect on page 44.
Illicit Pharmacology
The Illicit Pharmacology skill does not have a governing Attribute, 
and therefore does not have a skill cap.
Illicit Pharmacology is a skill that represents knowledge of the 
underground world of illicit substances and pharmaceuticals, 
gained through experience as a drug dealer or personal in-
volvement with addictive substances.  Characters with illicit 
pharmacology skills possess knowledge of various narcotics, 
stimulants, and other controlled substances, including their 
effects, production methods, distribution networks, and asso-
ciated risks.  This skill encompasses an understanding of drug 
Glossary
188


terminology, identification of different substances, knowl-
edge of their chemical compositions, and awareness of com-
mon methods of consumption and administration.  Characters 
with illicit pharmacology skills can assess the potency and 
quality of drugs, identify potential adulterants or dangerous 
additives, and gauge the potential risks and side effects as-
sociated with different substances.  Additionally, this skill may 
involve familiarity with the underground drug market, under-
standing the dynamics of drug transactions, and the ability to 
navigate drug-related subcultures.
Characters without the Illicit Pharmacology skill may still attempt 
these tasks with a Medical check, however the difficulty should be 
doubled, unless the Character is considered a Medical Professional.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Drug Addict/Alcoholic (page 
47) or Drug Dealer (page 47) Aspects.
Language (any)
The Language skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.
The ability to understand, speak, read, and write a specif-
ic language.  Characters with a 2 or higher in this skill can 
communicate effectively and fluently in the chosen language. 
They have a solid grasp of grammar, vocabulary, pronuncia-
tion, and idiomatic expressions, allowing them to interact with 
individuals who speak the same language.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Head of the Class (page 
45) Bilingual (page 45) Spec Ops (page 46) or Social 
Worker (page 46) Aspects.
Law
The Law skill does not have a governing Attribute, and therefore 
does not have a skill cap.
The knowledge and understanding of legal principles, reg-
ulations, and the legal system.  Characters with the Law skill 
are familiar with various aspects of the law, including criminal 
law, civil law, contract law, and more.  They have a grasp 
of legal procedures, rights, and responsibilities, which allows 
them to navigate legal situations, interpret laws, and even ex-
ploit laws.  The Law skill is useful for negotiating legal matters, 
providing legal advice, drafting contracts, conducting legal 
research, and understanding the implications of legal actions.
Characters without the Law skill may still attempt these tasks with a 
general Intellect check, however the information provided will be 
superficial at best.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Lawyer (page 45) or Crim-
inal Justice (page 44) Aspects.
Leadership
The Leadership skill does not have a governing Attribute, and 
therefore does not have a skill cap.
The Leadership skill is for guiding and inspiring others towards
