a common goal.  Characters with leadership skills possess the 
ability to take charge, make decisions, and effectively com-
municate their vision to others.  They have a natural charisma 
and influence that allows them to motivate and rally individu-
als or groups, fostering teamwork and cooperation.  Leader-
ship involves qualities such as clear communication, strategic 
thinking, empathy, and the ability to inspire confidence and 
trust in others.  Characters with high leadership skills excel 
at organizing and coordinating efforts, providing guidance, 
and resolving conflicts within a team or organization.
Characters without the Leadership skill may still attempt these tasks 
with a Persuade check, however the difficulty should be doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Business Management Aspect 
on page 44.
Physics
The Physics skill does not have a governing Attribute, and therefore 
does not have a skill cap.
The scientific study of matter, energy, motion, and the funda-
mental principles that govern the physical world.  Characters 
with the Physics skill possess knowledge of the laws of nature, 
the properties of matter, and the forces that shape the universe. 
They understand concepts such as gravity, electromagnetism, 
thermodynamics, and quantum mechanics. The Physics skill 
enables characters to analyze and predict the behavior of 
physical systems, calculate trajectories, determine the prop-
erties of materials, and solve complex problems using math-
ematical models.  This skill is essential for understanding the 
mechanics of objects and phenomena, leveraging scientific 
principles to devise innovative solutions, and comprehending 
the underlying principles behind anomalies or extraordinary 
phenomena encountered in the universe.
Characters without the Physics skill may still attempt these tasks with a 
Mathematics check, however the difficulty should be doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Physicist Aspect on page 45.
Premonition
The Premonition skill has a Skill Cap equal to your Character’s In-
tuition Skill.
The ability to anomalously predict the future or obtain visions 
of potential dangers that have not yet occurred.  The Director 
is encouraged to provide the information obtained in a cryptic 
and vague manner.  Premonitions occur when the Director 
dictates, but the PC may meditate to attempt to trigger more 
frequent premonitions.
Characters without the Premonition skill may not attempt to obtain 
premonitions.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Psychic Aspect on page 55.
Reality Bending
Unlike all other Written-In Skills, Reality Bending is governed by 
the Fate Attribute.  When spending Skill Points, your Reality Bend-
ing skill cannot exceed half of your Fate Rating. 
Reality Bending is a special skill reserved for Characters with 
abnormally high Hume levels.  It represents the ability to al-
ter the fabric of reality to ones desires through anomalous 
means.  For more information please see the Reality Bending 
and Thaumaturgy Chapter on page 127.  
Characters without the Reality Bending skill may not attempt to 
bend reality unless they are in very low Hume environments.
This skill can only be obtained by purchasing the Type Green 
Aspect on page 53.
Glossary
189


Repair
The Repair skill does not have a governing Attribute, and therefore 
does not have a skill cap.
The ability to fix and restore broken or malfunctioning objects, 
devices, and machinery.  Characters with repair skills possess 
the knowledge and practical expertise to assess, diagnose, 
and rectify mechanical, electrical, or structural issues.  They 
can use tools, spare parts, and their problem-solving abili-
ties to mend damaged equipment, repair vehicles, or restore 
functionality to broken systems.  Repair skills encompass a 
wide range of disciplines, including mechanical repairs, elec-
tronics, plumbing, carpentry, and more.
Characters without the Repair skill may still attempt these tasks with 
a Mechanics check, however the difficulty is doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Mechanic Aspect on page 45.
Telekinesis
The Telekinesis skill has a Skill Cap equal to your Character’s 
Self-Control Skill.
Extraordinary skill allowing the Character to manipulate ob-
jects or exert force without physical contact through the power 
of the mind.  With telekinesis, characters can move, lift, and 
control objects using sheer mental focus and concentration. 
They can push, pull, levitate, and even hurl objects at a dis-
tance, depending on the extent of their telekinetic abilities. 
This skill requires immense mental discipline and concentra-
tion to harness and direct the power effectively.
Characters without the Telekinesis skill can not attempt telekinesis.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Floating Blades (page 54) 
Levitate Other (page 55) or Telekinesis (page 56) Aspects.
Thaumatology
The Thaumatology Skill skill does not have a governing Attribute, 
and therefore does not have a skill cap.  Thaumatology uses the 
Anomalistics Skill Multiplier instead of it’s own.
Thaumatology is the ability to use and understand Thaumatur-
gy, an anomalous effect that manifests reality altering causal-
ities after performing certain rituals, incantations, and proce-
dures, typically found in ancient “spell books” or discovered 
through trial and error.  For more information please see the 
Reality Bending and Thaumatology Chapter on page 127. 
Characters without the Thaumatology skill can not attempt Thaumaturgy.
Thaumatology can be obtained by purchasing the Type Blue As-
pect on page 53, though it may be possible to learn Thaumatol-
ogy in-universe.  See also Thaumatology on page 130.
Tracking
The Tracking skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.
Tracking is a skill that represents the ability to follow and inter-
pret signs, clues, and tracks left behind by creatures or indi-
viduals.  It involves keen observation, understanding context 
clues, identifying footprints, analyzing scents, and interpret-
ing environmental cues.  Characters proficient in Tracking ex-
cel in finding hidden paths and uncovering the movements and 
intentions of targets through careful investigation of their trails. 
This skill is useful in both a wilderness and urban environments. 
Characters without the Tracking skill may still attempt these tasks 
with an Awareness check, however the difficulty should be doubled. 
Can be obtained by purchasing the Ecology Aspect on page 44.
Trivial Skill
The Trivial Skill skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.  This skills name is not written in as 
Trivial Skill, and instead is chosen by the player.
When a Trivial Skill is chosen, it must be a skill that is specif-
ic to a rare, single instance.  Good examples include Jug-
gling, Card Tricks, Video Game Mastery, or Movie Trivia. 
Characters without the Trivial skill in question may still attempt these 
tasks with a general Intellect check, but successes will not be as 
extravagant.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Strange Hobby Aspect on 
page 49.
Urban Survival
The Urban Survival skill does not have a governing Attribute, and 
therefore does not have a skill cap.
The ability to navigate and thrive in urban environments, 
utilizing knowledge and skills developed through personal 
experience in urban settings.  Characters with urban surviv-
al skills possess street smarts, understanding the dynamics 
of city life, and the ability to adapt and survive in diverse 
urban landscapes.  This skill encompasses knowledge of ur-
ban geography, familiarity with common urban hazards, un-
derstanding of local customs and slang, proficiency in find-
ing shelter & resources, and the ability to blend into urban 
crowds.  Urban survival skills enable characters to navigate 
through crowded streets, locate hidden pathways, identify 
safe or dangerous areas, scavenge for supplies, find tem-
porary shelter, and interact with various urban populations. 
Characters without the Urban Survival skill may still attempt these 
tasks with a Intuition check, however the difficulty is doubled.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Formerly Homeless Aspect on 
page 45.
Versatility
The Versatility skill does not have a governing Attribute, and there-
fore does not have a skill cap.
A unique skill exclusive to astronauts that represents adapt-
ability and a broad range of knowledge and abilities.  Char-
acters with the Versatility skill are highly adaptable and can 
use this skill in place of any other once per gaming session.
Can be obtained by purchasing the Astronaut Aspect on page 46.
Glossary
190


Characters
Advanced Character Creation
A three phase process that allows for the creation of a customized Character. 
Typically for use with PC (Player Characters) Creation.  Advanced Character 
Creation takes longer than the other two methods (Premade and Fast) but the 
ability to purchase Aspects and fine tune your Character will make it worth the 
time.  (Not suggested for first time players)
Age
For Fast Character Creation players may choose any Age they feel appropri-
ate for their Characters.  In Advanced Character Creation, Age is similar to 
your Disruption Class in that it will provide a similar baseline of stats.
Anomalous Aspect
An Aspect that provides a Character with a supernatural, magical, or other-
wise paranormal quality or ability.
Aspect
Purchasable only during Advanced Character Creation, an Aspect represents 
a historic detail or inherent quality about the Character which provides a 
bonus.  These bonuses can range from a package of skill bonuses, to unique 
combat qualities, or even anomalous superpowers.  Aspects can be pur-
chased using one or several of the Power, Talent, and History points obtained 
during Advanced Character Creation, often providing extra bonuses if multi-
ple points types are utilized.
Attribute Rating
The number that goes in the SCP icons to the top right of each of your Attri-
butes.  This number is a representation of each Die Icon that has been filled in 
(do not count Die Bumpers).  Attribute Ratings determine several other Sub-
stats, and can be used to determine Automatic Successes.
Attribute Token
A point type that can be used to purchase an Attribute Die Icon or a Die 
Bumper.
Base HP
Determined during Character Creation only, Base HP is the base number you 
start with when calculating your Hit Points (HP).
Base Speed
Determined during Character Creation only, Base Speed is the base number 
you start with when calculating your Move Speed.
Chapter Rewards/Chapter Bonuses
A package of bonuses determined by a Character’s Disruption Class.  Chapter 
Rewards are awarded to PCs at the end of each Chapter.
Chapters Survived
A tally of the number of Chapters a PC has survived, and used to keep track 
of their Power Level.
Character Creation
The process of creating stats and characteristics for a PC or NPC.
Combat X (Multiplier)
When calculating Melee Attack damage, you multiply your X Damage by 
your Combat Multiplier, i.e. Combat X.
Cost Icon
A circular icon for each Aspect that displays the cost to purchase it.  Black 
text on a white background notates a good value, while white text on a gray 
background is a premium price.  Aspects can be purchased with multiple point 
types to provide extra bonuses listed in italics.  Some of the bonuses listed can 
be utilized twice if all three point types are used.
Custom Meter
A few Aspects require the utilization of a Custom Meter.  This is a generic 
meter to be used for any purpose.  Characters are limited to two meters. 
DEX Divider
Your Dexterity Rating is divided by your DEX Divider (rounded up) to provide 
a bonus to your Move Speed.
Die Bumper
Die Bumpers are small circles located underneath some of the Attribute Die 
Icons on your Character Sheet.  Each circle requires one Attribute Token to fill 
in.  Dice located above the Die Bumpers cannot be purchased until the corre-
sponding Die Bumper is filled.
Disadvantages
Aspects that instead of providing bonuses to your Character, give penalties or 
hindrances  Instead of costing you Power, Talent, or History points, they give 
you points.  You are limited to a total of three Disadvantages per Character.
Disruption Class
In terms of the SCP Wiki, the Disruption Class is used to classify the level to 
which an SCP is able to cause panic or destruction throughout any given area 
if left unchecked.  For SCP.2e, this term also refers to the Character Creation 
step which determines a Character’s starting Power Level and Chapter Re-
wards.  Disruption Classes are a choice to be made which will determine if 
your Character will begin the game strong, weak, or something in between. 
The stronger you are from the beginning the less bonuses you’ll receive when 
you obtain Chapter Rewards.  Fast Character Creation always creates a Char-
acter with the Dark Disruption Class.  
• 
Dark - A Disruption Class which creates a very low level, or weak Char-
acter, however it has the best improvement earned each Chapter, which 
makes up for this early disadvantage.
• 
Vlam - A Disruption Class which creates somewhat weak Character, but 
has great improvement earned each Chapter, which can make up for this 
early disadvantage.
• 
Keneq - A Disruption Class which creates a moderately powerful start-
ing Character, and offers moderate Chapter Rewards.
• 
Ekhi - A Disruption Class which allows for a very powerful starting 
Character, however provides very small Chapter Rewards.
• 
Amida - A Disruption Class which allows for Characters to begin the 
game extremely powerful, however once the Character has been creat-
ed there is no additional improvement in the future.
Dividers
There are four Dividers on the SCP.2e Character sheet.  DEX, STR, PER, and 
WIL.  Typically these are determined by a Characters Disruption Class.  Each 
Divider is used to divide its corresponding Attribute Rating, providing values 
to Speed, Combat, Projectile, and Exertion stats respectively.
DR (Damage Reduction)
Just before Damage is applied to a Character, reduce the total applied by 
your Damage Reduction (DR).  DR can be obtained through Aspects, Uni-
forms, and Uniform Augmentations.
Exertion
An expendable resource Characters can use to add D12 bonuses to their Dice 
Pools, boost Speed, Combat, & Projectile ratings, can be spent to roll Dodge 
after you’ve taken a turn, as well as used with some Aspects.
Fast Character Creation
A simplified four step process to create a Dark Disruption Class Character 
using a class based system and preset Loadouts.
History points
A point value used in Advanced Character Creation.  The older a Character is, 
the higher History Points they will have.  History Points are most valuable when 
purchasing Aspects that increase your Skill Levels.
Glossary
191


HP
HP (Hit Points) represent a Character’s health or vitality.  As Characters take 
damage, their hit points decrease, and when they reach zero or less, Death 
Saves will determine if the Character survives the damage.
HTHx (Health Multiplier)
Stands for “Heath Multiplier.”  This rating is multiplied by your Health Rating 
and added to your Base HP to calculate your Max HP.
MAX HP
The most amount of HP a Character is allowed to have at any one time.  Char-
acters begin the game at maximum HP.
Mental
The bottom four Attributes are “Mental” Attributes, meaning they represent 
mental and social proficiency.  When referring to NPCs, Mental replaces all 
Mental based skills.
PER Divider
Your Perception Rating is divided by your PER Divider (rounded up) to calcu-
late your Projectile Rating.
Personnel Class
In terms of Character Creation, Personnel Class is usually going to be written 
in as “C” unless your Character is starting off as a D-Class Personnel.  Your 
Personnel Class represents how valuable a person is to the SCP Foundation. 
Higher Personnel Classes are not allowed to be exposed to anomalies.  PCs 
will typically remain as C Class the entire campaign.
Physical
The top four Attributes are “Physical” Attributes, meaning they represent brute 
force and bodily virtue.  When referring to NPCs, Physical replaces all Phys-
ical based skills.
Power points
A point value used in Advanced Character Creation.  The higher a Characters 
Disruption Class is, the more Power Points they will have.   Power Points are the 
most valuable point type in the game and are required if you want to purchase 
some of the most powerful (anomalous) Aspects.
Premade Characters
A list of 45 Character Sheets with all items, stats, and details already filled in. 
This pdf comes with the purchase of SCP.2e and is a free download at Drive-
thrurpg.com.  There are 9 Premade Characters for each of the 5 Disruption 
Classes.
Primary Benefit
When using Fast Character Creation, Classes will provide Characters with a 
Primary Benefit.  Primary Benefits are a series of skill bonuses and/or other 
abilities provided to the Character.
Projectile X (Multiplier)
When calculating Firearm Attack damage, you multiply your X Damage by 
your Projectile Multiplier, i.e. Projectile X.
Reverence
A meta-currency that is awarded by the Director for any action performed by 
a Player which provides a positive experience in role-playing.  Two Reverence 
can be spent to re-roll a Skill Check.  Can also be used to buy Drama Cards.
Skill Point
A point type that can be used to increase your Skill levels.  Skills cost a number 
of Skill Points equal to the level they are being increased to, but cannot exceed 
their Skill Cap.
Social
When referring to NPCs, Social replaces all Social based skills.
Speed
Rating which determines how many Units (meters) a Character can travel us-
ing the Move Combat Action.
STR Divider
Your Strength Rating is divided by your STR Divider (rounded up) to calculate 
your Combat Rating.
SubStats
A term referring to the stats on a character sheet that can not be directly in-
creased, and instead are determined by the level of other stats.  These include 
Speed, Combat Rating, Projectile Rating, Exertion, Max HP, and the Reaction 
& Stagger scores.
Talent points
A point value used in Advanced Character Creation.  The higher a Characters 
Disruption Class is, the more Talent Points they will have.   Talent Points are 
most valuable when purchasing Aspects that provide Combat Bonuses/Op-
tions or other miscellaneous abilities.
Title
A one or two-word description chosen by the Player to represent their 
Character’s role or profession.  Titles can range from specific job titles or 
Classes, to broader descriptions of the Character’s abilities or characteristics.
WIL Divider
Your Willpower Rating is divided by your WIL Divider (rounded up) +1 to 
calculate your total Exertion Points.
Combat
Acid
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Acid represents inju-
ry caused by chemicals or dangerous liquids.
Aim (Action)
When using Firearms, the Aim Action will provide a bonus to your next To-Hit 
roll equal to the weapons Aim stat.
Attack of Opportunity
When a Character leaves a Unit that is adjacent to an enemy, that enemy may 
perform a free Melee Attack.  You may avoid this rule by moving exactly one 
Unit with a Move Action.
Blunt
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Blunt represents 
injury caused by fists, clubs, or physical impact.
Called Shots
When performing an attack, a Called Shot allows Characters to target spe-
cific locations such as the head or legs.  Called Shots open up the options for 
several other game mechanics such as inducing Stagger or dealing double 
damage.
Cold
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Cold represents in-
jury caused by extremely low temperatures.
Combat Action
There are eight Combat Actions Characters can perform during Combat. 
Characters can perform up to two Combat Actions on each of their Turns.
Coup de Grâce
This term describes a decisive and final blow delivered to an enemy who is 
already incapacitated or near death.  Borrowed from French, translates to 
“blow of mercy” or “stroke of grace.”  In game mechanics, a Coup de Grâce 
is performed without the need for dice rolls or combat checks against oppo-
nents whom have obviously lost the Combat encounter, or against sleeping or 
unconscious Characters, allowing players to narratively describe how their 
Character dispatches a severely wounded or helpless opponent in a swift and 
efficient manner.  This mechanic is often used to streamline combat encounters 
and provide opportunities for dramatic storytelling moments when dealing 
with defeated foes.  It is similar to the Fatality mechanic and appointed by the 
Director to avoid unnecessary dice rolls.
Glossary
192


CPR
CPR, short for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation, can be performed with or 
without a Defibrillator.  This process allows Characters to attempt to save oth-
ers who have recently failed a Death Save.
Crouch
See Prone; Crouching is mechanically the same as being Prone.
Cutting
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Cutting represents 
injury caused by caused by sharp-edged or bladed weapons.
Damage Type
Also known as Damage Element, Damage Types are used to determine what 
type of Mutilation occurs.
Death Save
After a Character is reduced to below zero HP, they must roll Fate + Resist 
Death and score double the negative HP they have or die.  This roll is called 
a Death Save.
Defense (Action)
The Defense Action allows Characters to roll Dexterity + Dodge to determine 
their Defense Score against attacks that occur later in the Combat Round.
Defense Score
Whenever a Character attacks another, their To-Hit roll must meet or exceed 
the opponents Defense Score.  The Defense Score is usually equal to the Char-
acters Reaction score or a Dexterity + Dodge skill check, however this determi-
nation can be altered if the Character is Staggered or other system mechanics 
are involved.
Disarm (Action)
Disarm is a Combat Action that allows a Character to attempt to remove a 
weapon from the opponents hand.
Dodged Attacks
If an attack is Dodged, it means that a Character has rolled their Dexterity + 
Dodge skill and scored higher than the To-Hit roll of the Character attacking 
them.  Some Aspects specifically mention an attack must be “Dodged” to trig-
ger an effect.  Note that “Dodging” an attack is not the same as “Evading” an 
attack.  See also “Evade”.
Dual Wield
Characters are able to hold a weapon in one hand if their Strength Rating 
is equal to or greater than the weapon’s Mass.  If a weapon is held in each 
hand, this is referred to as Dual Wielding, and it opens up some additional 
bonuses and options.
Electricity
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Electricity represents 
injury caused by lighting and electrical discharge.
Enemy
See “Target”
Evade
When an attack is avoided, the attack is considered to have been evaded. 
Evade can occur both when a Character Dodges an attack, or if passively 
avoided through the Reaction Score.  See also “Dodge.”
Explosion
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Explosion represents 
injury caused by destructive forces unleashed by explosive devices or power-
ful detonations, such as Grenades or C4.
Extended Action
An action that requires more than one Combat Action to perform.  Extended 
Actions require two or more consecutive Combat Actions to pass before the 
Action can be attempted, representing a task that takes longer than normal 
to complete.
Fatality
When a Called Shot is successfully performed, a Fatality may be attempted.  If 
the enemy has half or less of their HP left, the Player may describe a gruesome 
execution.
Fire (Action)
This Combat Action is performed whenever a Character fires a gun at a Target. 
The Character rolls their Perception + Firearms and tries to meet or exceed the 
Target’s Defense Score.
Fire (Damage Type)
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Fire represents injury 
caused by extreme heat or fire.
Fire Intensity
If a Character becomes set on fire, they will have a status called “On Fire” 
applied to them along with a “Fire Intensity” level between 1 and 5.  At the 
end of each Combat Round, the Character on fire will take 1D10 damage for 
each level of Fire Intensity they have.
First Aid (Action)
This Combat Action can be performed with and without a First Aid Kit, and re-
stores some HP.  Providing First Aid to someone else leads to greater benefits.
Free Action
In reference to Combat, Free Actions are simple tasks that wouldn’t require a 
Combat Action.  Examples include speaking a sentence, turning ones head, or 
dropping a weapon to the ground.  Free Actions can only be used on your Turn. 
Free Movement
After any attack has been successfully avoided, Characters have the option 
to move 1 Unit in any direction.  This movement is referred to as “Free Move-
ment.”  Does not trigger Attacks of Opportunity and vice versa.
Independent Skill Check
Specific to how an Attack of Opportunity is resolved, an Independent Skill 
Check refers to a roll that ignores all other game states and modifiers.  For 
example, if a Character has already taken their Turn and their Defense Score 
is usually equal to their Reaction Score, an Independent Skill Check will su-
persede this and allow the defending Character to roll Dexterity + Dodge as if 
they have not yet acted that Round.  Additionally, any other modifiers to this 
versus check are void (Director’s discretion).
Melee Attack (Action)
This Combat Action is performed whenever a Character uses a Melee attack 
(fists, swords, etc.) against a Target.  The Character rolls their Dexterity + Me-
lee and tries to meet or exceed the Target’s Defense Score.
Modifiers
Whenever a Skill Check has additional bonuses or penalties to the roll besides 
just adding a Skill, it is referred to as a “Modifier.”
Momentum
When a Character travels one or more Units in a Combat Round, they build up 
Momentum, which can increase Melee damage.
Move (Action)
A Combat Action that allows a Character to travel a number of Units equal to 
their Speed Rating.
Multiple Attackers
If a single Character is being attacked by multiple Characters, apply a -2 
penalty to the Characters Defense Score for each instance of a new attacker 
throughout the Combat Round.
Mutilation
Players may opt to receive a Mutilation for any incoming damage.  Addi-
tionally, Characters must opt to receive a Mutilation if an attack reduces their 
HP below zero once each Combat.  Mutation effects are deterministic on the 
attacks Damage Type.
Glossary
193


Non-Standard Action
Any action a Character performs that is not covered in this rule book and must 
be improvised by the Director.
On Fire
A status effect that inflicts a Fire Intensity upon a Character.
Opponent
See “Target”
Overwatch
A special Combat Action which allows a Character to shoot a firearm at any-
one whom passes through a designated area in their sights.
Piercing
One of the eight Damage Types, aka Damage Elements.  Piercing represents 
injury caused by stabbing weapons or bullets.
Poisoned
A status effect which simulates a Character experiencing a sickness or poisoning.
Prone
Also known as Crouching (or Crouch/Prone) is a state or stance a Character 
can be in that lowers their profile.  This state limits some of the Combat Ac-
tions available to you but increases your Defense Score by 2 against Firearms. 
Characters must be in the Crouch/Prone position in order to fire Sniper Rifles.
Prone position reduces Fire Intensity by 3 after the damage is taken.
Reaction Score
When a Character is attacked after they have taken their Turn, the Defense 
Score of the defender becomes their Reaction score.
Reload (Action)
Once a firearm has depleted the ammunition within it’s magazine, the Reload 
Combat Action must be performed to provide the weapon with a fresh clip.
Restrain (Action)
This Combat Action allows Characters to attempt to restrain others, preventing 
them from taking further Combat Actions.
Severity Level (Mutilation)
Mutilations are determined randomly by rolling 1D12.  The higher the num-
ber rolled on this D12 will yield a more severe Mutilation, referred to as it’s 
Severity Level.
Short Rest
Characters may rest for 30 minutes to restore all lost Exertion and HP equal to 
their Health Rating.  Short Rests may be performed twice daily.
Sleeping
Each night Characters may spend 8 hours to sleep.  Sleeping restores all Exer-
tion and restores HP equal to a Health + Resilience Skill Check.
Spray & Pray
A special Combat Action which allows a Character using an Automatic Fire-
arm to shoot several Targets within a designated area using only one Combat 
Action.
Stagger
A state that represents a Character whom is off balance and unable to Dodge 
incoming attacks.  To-Hit rolls against a Staggered Character have a difficulty 
equal to the Character’s Stagger score (Or Reaction score if lower).
Standard Action
Any Combat Action that is outlined within this rule book.  Typically referring to 
the eight Standard Combat Actions.
Standard Combat Procedure
Referring to the standard use of Combat Mechanics outlined in the Combat 
Chapter of this book.
Target
Sometimes referred to as “the enemy” or “the opponent” a Target is the intend-
ed focus of an offensive action, typically in reference to another Character. 
To-Hit
Whenever a Character attempts to attack another, they must roll a Skill Check 
against their opponents Defense Score.  This Skill Check is often referred to as 
the “To-Hit” roll.  Weapons also have a “To-Hit” stat, which is added to the 
To-Hit roll when attacking with that weapon.
Turn
During each Combat Round, Characters take two Combat Actions, collec-
tively referred to as their ‘Turn.’  Once a Character completes their Turn, the 
controlling player selects the next Character to act.
Unarmed
In reference to a Character who is not equipped with a weapon, or uses bare 
hands and kicks for dealing damage.
Unconscious
A status effect which renders a Character asleep.
Use Item (Action)
A generic Combat Action for any stationary use of an item.
Game System
Attribute Check
See “Skill Check”
Attribute Dice
A set of various dice that represent the aptitude for any given Attribute.  Dice 
are represented using Attribute Dice Icons.  The dice you roll are referred to as 
a Dice Pool, or Die Pool.  Each die is represented as one column.
Automatic Successes
Players may opt to not roll Attribute Dice, and instead treat their Attribute 
Rating as the sum of their top two dice.  This can lead to Automatic Successes 
without the need to roll dice.
Bonuses
Can refer to a Director providing a plus to a Skill Check due to a situational 
circumstance, or refers to the bonus that a Skill can provide.  For example, a 
skill of 3 with a Multiplier of x2 would provide a “bonus” of +6.
Campaign Diversity Level (CDL)
The Campaign Diversity Level (CDL) is a shorthand indicator that tells Players 
what Disruption Classes the Director is allowing for any given campaign.
Campaign Power Level
Your Campaign Power Level refers to the intended level of strength for the 
Party, and is decided by the Director.
Chapter
Chapters typically refer to “one gaming session” however the Director is in 
charge of when a Chapter ends.  At the end of each Chapter, Chapter Re-
wards are given.
Crafting Weapons
Players may attempt to create makeshift weaponry.  This process is referred to 
as Crafting Weapons.
Critical Failure
When a Skill Check results in a Score that is half of the Difficulty (rounded 
down), then a Critical Failure occurs.
Critical Success
When a Skill Check results in a Score double or more than the Difficulty, then 
a Critical Success occurs.
Glossary
194


Damage Element
See [Combat] Damage Type
Die Pool
When you gather dice to roll for an Attribute, the dice are collectively referred 
to as your Die Pool.
Difficulties
When a Skill Check is performed, the Director will often provide a Difficulty. 
The sum of your top two dice and skill bonus must meet or exceed the Difficulty 
in order to succeed the Skill Check.
Drama Cards
You can buy a deck of Drama Cards at Drivethrurpg.com.  A Drama Card 
can be purchased in-game with Reverence Points and allow Players to trigger 
special rules and abilities to surprise the Director.
Dual Tech
Two Characters may attempt to perform a single task in unison.  This is referred 
to as a Dual Tech.
Experience
See [Characters] Chapter Rewards.  Many Tabletop RPGs use “Experience 
Points” as a system for progression.  SCP.2e uses Chapter Rewards.
Exploding Dice
When Attribute Dice are rolled and a die rolls it’s maximum result, you are 
allowed to roll one die of a higher type and include it in your Die Pool.  This 
system is referred to as ‘Exploding dice’.
Favors
When a PC performs a requested task by an NPC, a Favor Point may be 
awarded to the Character or party.  These points can be exchanged for vari-
ous requests, objects, and rewards.
General Attribute Check
When no skill is appropriate, Directors can request a General Attribute Check, 
which has no skill added to the top two dice rolled.  Typically, with a slightly 
reduced difficulty.
Governing Attribute
Skills often have a “Governing Attribute” which is a commonly used Attribute 
when performing that skill’s Check.  Skills can not go higher than half their 
Governing Attributes Rating (rounded up) when spending Chapter Reward 
Skill Points.
Jumping
Characters can jump horizontally a distance equal to their (Speed Rating + 
Athletics Skill) divided by 2, rounded down.  This distance divided in half will 
provide the Characters vertical jump height.
Medical Professional
Often times a rule set may limit medical procedures to Characters that have 
obtained a Medical Professional label.  These Characters have had extensive 
training in the medical field and are able to attempt difficult tasks such as sur-
geries, or mending broken bones.
Multiplier
Can refer to one of the two Damage Multipliers on the Character Sheet (Com-
bat X and Projectile X) or refer to a Skill Multiplier.
Object Mass
A rating that determines the weight and size of an object.
Power Levels
Refers to the overall combat ability or quality of stats for a Character.  Can 
also refer to the type of campaign a Director intended to run in terms of PC 
strength.
Punishing Roll
Notably difficult Skill Checks can be designated by the Director as Punish-
ing Rolls, or a Punished Skill Check.  This means that Exertion and Reverence 
cannot be used on the roll, dice do not Explode, and Automatic Successes 
are not available.
Radiation
An emission of energy as electromagnetic waves or as moving subatomic par-
ticles, especially high-energy particles which cause ionization.
Random Difficulty
Directors can opt to generate random difficulties by totaling a set of 3 or more dice. 
Sanity
The ability to think and behave in a rational manner.  Sanity Checks (Intellect 
+ Self-Control) can be requested by the Director if Characters witness extreme 
horrors.
Score
When you roll Attribute Dice, you add the top two dice from the pool of dice, 
and add your Skill.  The total of these numbers is referred to as your Score.
SCP Stats
Shorthand list of stats for an anomalous entity, typically one that is referenced 
in the SCP Wiki.
Security Level
Personnel that work with or for the SCP Foundation usually have a Security 
Level (Between 0 and 5) to determine their level of access to locations and 
information.  Security Levels are also used for the SCP.2e Drama Card Deck, 
which determines how many Reverence Points are required for purchase.
Skill Cap
The maximum level that a Skill can be increased to using Skill Points is equal to 
half of its Governing Attribute’s Rating.  Note that Skills may exceed this level 
during Character Creation if the Skill is exclusively increased using Aspects.
Skill Multiplier
Some Aspects will allow a Skill to have a Skill Multiplier, typically when multi-
ple point types are spent into them.  Multipliers multiply the Skill before it is add-
ed to the top two dice in a Skill Check.  Skill Multipliers can never exceed x3. 
Skill Check
Also known as an Attribute Check, the process of rolling dice for one Attri-
bute, adding the top two dice, and finally adding a Skill.
Skills
A list of various aptitude/talent scores that are added to the top two dice of a 
Dice Pool when performing a Skill Check.
Splash Damage
When an attack deals damage not only to the intended Target, but also any 
Characters nearby.
Structure Rating
A rating that describes the sturdiness of a structure, such as a wall or wooden door. 
Substats
Stats for a Character that can not be increased directly and instead are calcu-
lated in relation to another stat or stats.
Throwing Objects
A system for how to determine what an object or weapon hits when it is thrown 
at a Target or Unit.
Timed Rolls
Similar to Extended Actions, Timed Rolls represent an action or series of ac-
tions that require a very long time to complete.  Directors can slowly reveal 
the difficulty of these tasks to simulate a slow discovery or gradual challenges.
Glossary
195


Triple Tech
Three Characters may attempt to perform a single task in unison.  This is re-
ferred to as a Triple Tech.
Foundation Presence Level
A set of canon universe types that model various degrees of Foundation pres-
ence throughout your game world.
Versus Rolls
When two Characters perform Skill Checks against each other, it is referred 
to as a Versus Roll.
Written In Skills
Skills Characters can obtain that are written down on the Character sheet as 
they are not in the pregenerated list.
RPG Terminology
Buy-In
A term that refers to how much your Players are invested in your game uni-
verse and have a sense that the world is real.
Campaign
A series of gaming sessions set in the same world, typically unified with an 
overarching story-line.
Character
Characters are fictional people and beings that are roleplayed as in your fic-
tional Campaign, typically with stats associated with them.  PC are Charac-
ters controlled by the Players, while NPCs are Characters controlled by the 
Director.
Director (GM/DM)
The person who governs and referees the game, plans out a storyline or series 
of interesting events, and roleplays all NPCs.
Metagame/Metagaming
The act of using knowledge or information that a player has, but their char-
acter would not logically possess, to influence actions or decisions within the 
game universe.  This includes any actions taken based on out-of-character 
information, rather than what the character would realistically know or do 
based on their in-game experiences and abilities.
NPC (Non-Player Characters)
Characters controlled by the Director.
Party
A term referring to all of the PCs (Player Characters) as a group.
PC (Player Characters)
Characters controlled by the Players.
Player
Refers to the actual real-world people that take on the role of PCs.
SCP.2e
Shorthand for SCP The Tabletop Role-Playing Game Second Edition, a game 
by 26letterpublishing (i.e. this book).
SCP Universe
A5 Armor
Advanced Foundation Armor used for dire situations.  A5 Armor is the best Com-
bat Uniform in this game, and is similar to common science fiction power armor. 
Akiva Radiation
Per the SCP Wiki:  [Akiva Radiation is] an anomalous field that fluctuates based 
on human belief and divine intervention, possible to measure and quantify in 
centiAkiva (cAk). High levels of Akiva radiation correlate with the divine pres-
ence in a location, the piety of an individual, and the religious significance of 
objects. Named after a Jewish scholar and religious leader, the Rabbi Akiva. 
Amnestic
Per the SCP Wiki:  [Amnestics are] drugs, procedures, or devices that induce 
memory loss when administered to an individual. While sedatives with amnes-
tic properties are not unknown to medical science, the Foundation’s amnestics 
have much greater potency and specificity, enabling them to make civilian 
witnesses forget about anomalous activity. 
Antimeme
A concept or idea which anomalously self-censors itself to prevent widespread 
knowledge.
Anomaly/Anomalous
Per the SCP Wiki: [Anomaly is a] Foundation term for the objects they con-
tain and research. Generally defined as anything that cannot be explained 
by current scientific knowledge, behaving in a manner contrary to otherwise 
universal laws and principles.
Apollyon
Apollyon is a Containment Classification.  Per the SCP Wiki: Apollyon-class 
SCPs are anomalies that cannot be contained, are expected to breach con-
tainment imminently, or some other similar scenario. Such anomalies are usu-
ally associated with world-ending threats or a K-Class Scenario of some kind, 
and require a massive effort from the Foundation to deal with.
Archon
Archon is a Containment Classification.  Per the SCP Wiki: Archon-class SCPs 
are anomalies that could theoretically be contained but are best left uncon-
tained for some reason. Archon SCPs may be a part of consensus reality that 
is difficult to fully contain, or may have adverse effects if put into containment. 
These SCPs are not uncontainable - the defining feature of the class is that the 
Foundation chooses to not put the anomaly into containment.
Canons
Canon refers to the body of official or accepted writings, stories, or interpre-
tations within the SCP Universe.  Different Canons may present alternative or 
complementary views of the SCP Foundation and its universe, offering diverse 
perspectives on the nature of anomalies, narrative themes, and how many/
which SCPs exist within it.
Cognitive Resistance Value (CRV)
Directors can determine this optional value by adding a Character’s Intellect 
Rating plus Self-Control skill.  Per the SCP Wiki:  [Cognitive Resistance Value 
is] a measurement of the mental fortitude and resistance of an individual to 
mind-affecting anomalies, particularly those that are memetic.  
Cognitohazard
Per the SCP Wiki: [Cognitohazard is] a term used to refer to objects that are 
dangerous to perceive. This could occur through any or all of the human sens-
es, including sight, sound, smell, taste, or touch. Differs from an infohazard in 
that simply being informed about a cognitohazard has no anomalous effect. 
Containment Breach
Per the SCP Wiki:  [Containment Breach is a] Term used when an SCP 
object escapes containment. Could involve physically breaking out of a 
containment chamber, could also mean spread of knowledge about it 
outside of the Foundation.
Glossary
196


Containment Chamber
Per the SCP Wiki:  [A Containment Chamber is] a room dedicated to the con-
tainment of an SCP object [or entity].
Containment Class
Also known as Object Class, Per the SCP Wiki:  A system of categorization 
based on the difficulty of containing the entity or object. [...] definitions of the 
most commonly used object classes (Safe, Euclid, Keter), as well as some less 
commonly used object classes (including Thaumiel, Neutralized and Apol-
lyon). More esoteric object classes may replace these, and supplementary 
classification systems may be used (such as adding a Threat Level, or using 
the Anomaly Classification System, which replaces “Object Class” with “Con-
tainment Class”).
D-Class
D-Class personnel, often referred to as Disposable Class, are individuals 
typically drawn from convicted criminals, death row inmates, or individuals 
from impoverished backgrounds who are utilized by the SCP Foundation for 
dangerous experiments, tests, and containment procedures involving anoma-
lies.  D-Class personnel are considered expendable and are used for tasks that 
carry significant risk to life and safety.  Players can begin the game as D-Class 
Personnel by choosing the D-Class “Class” in Fast Character Creation or by 
taking the D-Class Disadvantage Aspect.
Dr. Wondertainment
Dr. Wondertainment is a mysterious and enigmatic figure or entity within the 
SCP Universe, often associated with the creation of anomalous objects, toys, 
and products designed for children.  These items are known for their fantastical 
and sometimes dangerous properties.  The true nature and motivations of Dr. 
Wondertainment remain unclear, and the entity’s creations often defy conven-
tional logic and understanding.
Elan-Vital Energy
Per the SCP Wiki:  [Elan-Vital Energy is] a mysterious energy emitted from liv-
ing beings and sapient anomalies that can power thaumatology. [...] May be 
referred to by alternative terms, such as Aura, Mana, Orgone or Qi (Chi/Ki).
Euclid
Euclid is a Containment Classification.  Per the SCP Wiki: Euclid-class SCPs 
are anomalies that require more resources to contain completely or where 
containment isn’t always reliable. Usually this is because the SCP is insuffi-
ciently understood or inherently unpredictable. Euclid is the Object Class with 
the greatest scope, and it’s usually a safe bet that an SCP will be this class if it 
doesn’t easily fall into any of the other standard Object Classes.  As a note, 
any SCP that’s autonomous or sapient is generally classified as Euclid, due 
to the inherent unpredictability of an object that can act or think on its own.
Groups of Interest (GoI)
Per the SCP Wiki:  A Group of Interest is an organization other than the Foun-
dation that is aware of the anomalous.
Hume
Per the SCP Wiki:  [A Hume is] a unit used to measure the strength or amount 
of reality in an area. [...] Named for the philosopher David Hume.
Infohazard
Per the SCP Wiki: [Infohazard is] a term used to refer to objects that have an 
anomalous effect whenever they are referred to or described. Differs from a 
cognitohazard in that cognitohazards require the anomalous phenomenon to 
be directly perceived, whereas infohazards may be spread indirectly, simply 
through people telling each other about them. Because of this, the effects of 
infohazards are often memetic.
K-Class Scenarios
Per the SCP Wiki: [K-Class Scenarios are] hypothetical situations with drastic 
effects on normality or reality. The most well-known K-Class Event, the XK-Class 
Event, typically denotes a catastrophic event resulting in the destruction of hu-
man society, if not the entire human species, but various other scenarios have 
their own designations, used with varying consistency across different articles. 
Kant Counter
Per the SCP Wiki: [A Kant Counter is an] instrument used to measure the Hume 
level of a location, that is, the amount of reality that is present. [...] Named for 
the philosopher Immanuel Kant.
Keter
Keter is a Containment Classification.  Per the SCP Wiki: Keter-class SCPs are 
anomalies that are exceedingly difficult to contain consistently or reliably, with 
containment procedures often being extensive and complex. The Foundation 
often can’t contain these SCPs well due to not having a solid understanding 
of the anomaly, or lacking the technology to properly contain or counter it. 
A Keter SCP does not mean the SCP is dangerous, just that it is simply very 
difficult or costly to contain.
Liminal Spaces
Term referring to an anomalous location which repeats it’s architecture for a 
seemingly infinite distance.
Meme/Memetics
Per the SCP Wiki:  Memes are the basic units of culture, ideas or behaviors 
that can be transmitted to others through communication or imitation. In the 
context of the Foundation, memetic effects are a sub-class of cognitohazards 
and infohazards that deal with the transfer of information. Ideas and concepts 
with anomalous memetic properties can spread much more effectively than 
non-anomalous memes, and may have anomalous effects on anyone exposed 
to them. Creators and users of anomalous memetic effects may be referred to 
as memeticists or cryptomancers.
Memetic Kill Agent
Per the SCP Wiki: A memetic hazard with a lethal effect on anyone exposed 
to it without the appropriate inoculation with a countermeme. Used to protect 
SCP-001 and other important data from unauthorized access.
Mobile Task Force (MTF)
Per the SCP Wiki:  [Mobile Task Forces (MTF) are] highly-trained and special-
ized team that is deployed in the field by the Foundation to deal with specific 
threats or conditions. Mobile Task Forces are designated by a Greek letter 
and a number (e.g. “MTF Alpha-7”, “MTF Omega-15”) and may have a 
nickname attached, similar to many real-life military units. MTFs are the elite 
personnel of the Foundation, and run the gamut from highly experienced field 
researchers to combat-hardened troops, with each MTF specializing in a dif-
ferent scenario or class of anomaly. Other types of Task Force also exist. [...]
O5 Council
Per the SCP Wiki:  Short for the “Council of Observers, Level 5” or the “Over-
seer Level 5 Council”. The highest authority in the Foundation and the leading 
body of Overwatch Command, a council of 12 or 13 individuals who have the 
power to authorize or veto any action taken by the Foundation as a whole. 
O5 Council members are only known by their numbers (O5-1 through O5-
13), are rarely involved in day-to-day Foundation activities, and are typical-
ly not allowed to contact any SCP objects directly for fear of contamination. 
(Note that it’s a capital letter “o”, not the number zero.)
Object Class
See Containment Class.
Pluripotent Entities
Per the SCP Wiki:  An incredibly powerful anomalous entity, possibly omnip-
otent. [...]
Reality Bending
The ability to alter reality through sheer will of an individual, typically those 
with high Hume levels.  These individuals are often referred to as Type Greens.
Glossary
197


Safe
Safe is a Containment Classification.  Per the SCP Wiki: Safe-class SCPs are 
anomalies that are easily and safely contained. This is often due to the fact that 
the Foundation has researched the SCP well enough that containment does 
not require significant resources or that the anomalies require a specific and 
conscious activation or trigger. Classifying an SCP as Safe, however, does not 
mean that handling or activating it does not pose a threat.
SCP
Abbreviation for Secure, Contain, Protect.  The three principles of the SCP 
Foundation.  Additionally, SCP is also the term used to describe anomalous 
objects and entities, or refer to the Foundation’s Special Containment Pro-
cedures.  In SCP.2e it can also refer to a Characters Speed, Combat, and 
Projectile substats.
SCP Foundation
An international secret organization dedicated to securing, containing, and 
protecting anomalous objects, entities, and phenomena, collectively referred 
to as SCPs.  The Foundation employs various methods to keep anomalous 
threats hidden from the public and to prevent them from causing harm or dis-
rupting normalcy.  It conducts research, containment, and experimentation on 
SCPs to better understand and manage them, often at great risk to its personnel. 
SCP Wiki
A website that collects user submitted writings to build a Creative Commons 
Licensed fictional universe themed in science fiction horror.  Inspired by a 
/x/4Chan post of the original SCP-173 (by Moto42) in 2007.  
https://scp-wiki.wikidot.com
Scranton Reality Anchors
Per the SCP Wiki: [The Scranton Reality Anchor is] a technology that stabilizes 
reality around it, preventing or restricting reality bending and anomalous in-
terference. Occasionally called a Scranton Box, although that may refer to a 
somewhat different technology. May function through the use of the captured 
reality benders, although there are alternative explanations for how they 
work. [...] Named after the in-universe inventor, one Robert Scranton.
Security Clearance
See [Game System] Security Level.
Spatial Humes
Humes that exist in a region or space, as opposed to in relation to a person or 
object.  Natural Spatial Hume levels average around 100±20.
Thaumatology/Thaumaturgy
Per the SCP Wiki: The scientific study of thaumaturgy, a term meaning “won-
der-working” and referring to magic, miracles and the occult. Users of magic 
can be referred to as thaumatologists, thaumaturges or “Type Blues” in Glob-
al Occult Coalition terminology, although more archaic terms like “wizard”, 
“sorcerer” and “mage” may still be used. When considered as distinct from 
reality bending, the phenomena studied by thaumatology generally follow 
more predictable rules and limitations.
Thaumiel
Thaumiel is a Containment Classification.  Per the SCP Wiki: Thaumiel-class 
SCPs are anomalies that the Foundation specifically uses to contain other 
SCPs. Even the mere existence of Thaumiel-class objects is classified at the 
highest levels of the Foundation, and their locations, functions, and current 
status are known to few Foundation personnel outside of the O5 Council.
The Administrator
Per the SCP Wiki:  [The Administrator is a] mysterious figure in-universe, may 
(or may not) have been the Founder of the SCP Foundation. Possibly a mem-
ber of the O5 Council, possibly the overall leader of the Foundation.
The Foundation
See SCP Foundation.
The Veil
Per the SCP Wiki:  A term referring to the separation of the anomalous and the 
people aware of it from the rest of the world. Actively maintained by Normal-
cy Organizations like the Foundation that conceal the existence of anomalies. 
a.k.a. The Veil/The Veil of Secrecy/The Veil of Normalcy/The Veil of Nor-
mality/The Veil Protocol.
The Wanderer’s Library
Per the Wanderer’s Library Hub:  The Wanderer’s Library is the hub of all 
knowledge throughout every universe and dimension. Its endless halls carry 
every book that has or ever will be written (and several that won’t). Scholars 
from worlds unknown stalk its aisles and trash bins for forbidden knowledge. It 
would take several lifetimes to read the contents of even one shelf in the Library. 
See also wanderers-library.wikidot.com
Ticonderoga
Ticonderoga is a Containment Classification.  Per the SCP Wiki: Ticondero-
ga-class SCPs cannot be contained, but also do not need to be contained. 
While these anomalies are similar to Archon-class SCPs in that their contain-
ment is unnecessary, Ticonderoga-class SCPs are distinguished by also not 
being containable given the Foundation’s current knowledge and resources. 
This may be due to their widespread or ubiquitous occurance on Earth, despite 
the general public remaining unaware of their existence or anomalous nature, 
although the Ticonderoga class also includes extraterrestrial anomalies be-
yond the current reach of the Foundation.
Type Blue
A practitioner of Thaumaturgy.
Type Green
An individual capable of Reality Bending in areas with average/normal Spa-
tial Hume levels, due to the high levels of Humes within them.
Type Red
A type of anomalous humanoid that has a rapid regenerative property and 
thus difficult to harm or kill.
Weapons & Items
Aim (Weapon Stat)
A weapon stat that provides a bonus To-Hit when the Aim Combat Action is 
performed.  See also [Combat] Aim (Action).
Base Damage
A weapon stat that determines the baseline (or minimum) damage the weapon 
will deal upon a successful attack.
Draw Actions
A Uniform stat that determines how many Combat Actions are required to 
draw a weapon out of it’s Weapon Slot.
Every Day Carry (EDC)
A list of common items that a PC keeps on their person at all times, even after 
changing into a different Uniform.
First Aid (Item)
A one-time-use item that can be used to increase the healing capability when 
using the First Aid Action.
Item Slot
A space on the front of your Character Sheet under Every Day Carry, which 
can hold commonly used items.
Loadouts
Term referring to a preset bundle of items and weapons associated with a 
Character Class.  Note that you may utilize Loadouts even when using Ad-
vanced Character Creation by copying a Class Loadout.
Glossary
198


Mag
Short for “Magazine.”  This stat tells you how many rounds can be fired from 
a gun before it requires the Reload Action.
Mass
Term that means the size and weight of something.  See also Weapon Mass, 
or Object Mass.
Outfit
See Uniform.
Range
A weapon stat that determines how far a weapon can be fired before it gets 
penalties To-Hit.  Each time the number of Units exceeds the weapons Range, 
reduce To-Hit by one.  An asterisk (*) denotes that the weapon can not be 
used past the noted range.
Recoil
If a weapon is fired more than once in the same Combat Round, the To-Hit roll 
is reduced by its recoil.
Slot Size
Uniforms may provide one or more Slots for Weapons.  The size of these slots 
limit what weapons can be stored within them based on the weapons Mass.
SUS
Short for “Suspicious Uniform Score” this rating is subtracted from all Charis-
ma rolls, and determines how much a Character stands out as a threat to the 
general public.
To-Hit (Weapon Stat)
Weapons have a “To-Hit” stat which is always added to the Skill Check when 
attempting to shoot or strike a Target.
Uniform
The clothing a Character is wearing, which determines the Characters SUS 
and Weapon Slots.
Uniform Augmentations
Modifications that can be added to a Uniform to alter their benefits and penalties. 
Uniform Style
There are many Uniforms to choose from.  This selection is referred to as a 
Character’s Uniform Style (A or B) on the back of your Character Sheet.
Weapon Mass
A weapon stat that determines how large and heavy the weapon is.  Weapons 
can not fit into Weapon Slots if their Mass is greater than the size of the slot.
Weapon Slot
A pocket within a Uniform that conceals a weapon from view.  Weapon Slots 
determine Draw Actions and Slot Size.
X Damage
When a weapon deals damage, the X Damage is multiplied by a Character’s 
Combat Rating (for Melee attacks) or Projectile Rating (for Firearms).  This is 
added to the Base Damage of a weapon to determine the final damage output. 
Glossary
199
